I finally got [my files](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emsenn-s-files.md) uploaded to [emsenn.net](../../07/19/2025-07-19-index.md)! This is a big step in the [Relaunch of emsenn.net](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/28/2025-08-28-wsl-backup-dev-relationality-content-triage-per-enc-2025-relaunch-of-emsenn-net.md), and bringing myself back to the [Web](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/economics-and-business-science-services-section/services/business-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/web.md) in a way that I'm comfortable with, and feels aligned with my position and orientation, as best I could describe those things.

I've babbled a lot about picking what tools to use to write and publish, but figured I'd give myself this space to discuss it as-it-is, right now.

Consistently for the past few years, I've structured my personal website as a [digital garden](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/digital-garden.md), influenced by [note-taking systems](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/note-taking-system.md) like [Zettelkasten](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/zettelkasten.md). There are a lot of individual pages, and then pages that list other pages. For example, there's this babble, and [my blog](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/my-blog.md) (my babble-log), which lists all my public babbles.

I'm using [Emacs](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emacs.md) as my [text editor](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text-editor.md), using a custom configuration I've named [GnoponEmacs](../08/2025-08-08-gnoponemacs.md) - a [neologism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/neologism.md) from gnosis ([Ancient Greek](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/ancient-greek-language.md) for *to know*), ponos (*to toil*), and Emacs.

A big part of that is using [Org-mode](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-mode.md) and [Org-roam](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-roam.md), two powerful and related modules that extend Emacs toward creating complex documents and interlinking, respectively.

Org-mode is like Markdown, in that it's a way of adding formatting to [plain text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/plain-text.md). But because it's built ontop of a specific piece of software, Emacs, it is able to do a lot more to let that markup be computable. Additionally, Org-mode has components for managing an agenda, templating the creation and modification of files, and notably, publishing files to other formats, including HTML.

Org-roam lets me build a database of a selection of Org-mode files, more readily working with some of that data. That's what lets me quickly find and insert links to other files in things like this babble, and build catalogs like my blog listing.

All this is a bit hard to explain, in this sort of babble, where I'm babbling with the intention that it be read by any random visitor to my website - who might be an expert Emacs user, but more likely hasn't ever used it, or probably even heard of it.

So, shifting gears a bit, let me just say: I'm using this specific set of software to take notes and publish my Website. The main page for that software is at [GnoponEmacs](../08/2025-08-08-gnoponemacs.md), for the nerds who wanna read more about it. (That's another perk of lots of pages: I can just, redirect the reader, as the writer.)

You might've noticed that a lot of the links that are in this babble don't appear to go anyplace, and if they do go someplace, they go to a mostly-empty page.

That's because this version of my digital garden, I only started working on in early August, 2025, after I finally upgraded [my laptop](../../../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/biographies/emsenn/emsenns-omnibook.md) to something better than the lowest-class [Chromebook](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/chromebook.md) available in 2019.

That old Chromebook (which is currently streaming trash TV as I babble and tidy my house) was what I got to replace my last "real" computer, which broke catastrophically when I was doing backups.

Because of that, I've lost a *lot* of my writing and research archive, and what I do have, is fragmented across different formats, like Markdown. I've got a goal of bringing that stuff into [my files](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emsenn-s-files.md), but it's going to take time. And so a part of that is using babbles to populate [nodes](../../../../../../../../../natural-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/node.md) that will help fill in the gaps between stuff.

So, let's see… freshly updated Website, returning to high-quality [free open-source software](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/free-open-source-software.md), and working to create this network of files…

Yeah - let me move toward explaining what I'm doing with why there's *so many* links - and in fact, I'm only adding as many as I feel like, not as many as there really *should* be in a text like this.

People who've only known me the past few years might not know, but when I was younger, I spent a lot more time exploring computers and programming.

In fact, this might be a good time to babble what has become somewhat of a formulaic babble from me, but a quick search shows there's no version of it in my current files:

Before computers could do graphics, it was all text: You'd type commands, and the computer would type things back at you. (In the Beginning… was the Command Line, by [Neal Stephenson](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/neal-stephenson.md) is a good (but out-of-date) essay about these [interfaces](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/command-line-interface.md), early [graphical user interface](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/graphical-user-interface.md), and the significantly different [user experience](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/user-experience.md) of the two.)

Of course, as soon as they could, people started making games that worked that way. [Zork](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/services/role-playing-games-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/zork.md) is an early [text-interface adventure game](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text-interface-adventure-game.md) that was many folks introduction to the concept. And, of course, as soon as [networked computing](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/networked-computing.md) came around, people started making, and playing, [multi-user text-interface games](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/multi-user-text-interface-game.md). Coming from folk who already loved playing [tabletop war games](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/services/role-playing-games-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/tabletop-war-game.md) and [tabletop role-playing games](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/services/role-playing-games-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/tabletop-role-playing-game.md), the first [major genre](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/major-genre.md) of these games were called [multi-user dungeons](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/multi-user-dungeon.md), or MUDs. (These days, that same acronym often gets expanded as [multi-user dimension](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/multi-user-dimension.md), as the genre developed significantly past its [role-playing game](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/services/role-playing-games-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/role-playing-game.md) roots.

When I was a young, a few things happned:

- I was born.
  - This was the condition for being young.
  - Notably, it happened in [Switzerland](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/switzerland.md), in 1990, so I was mostly exposed to [Schwyzerdütsch](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/schwyzerdutsch.md) (Swiss-German), along with a lot of [German](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/german-language.md), [French](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/french-language.md), and some [English](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/english-language.md) and [Italian](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/italian-language.md).
- About [four years later](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/1994.md), I was moved to the [United States of America](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/services/work-science-services-section/services/governance-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/united-states-of-america.md).
  - [General American English](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/general-american-english.md) became the main language around me.
- I also got a [personal computer](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/personal-computer.md), a [desktop](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/desktop-computer.md), which was quite a thing to have as a child at that time.
  - I even had [Internet](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/services/computer-web-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/internet.md) access. (*Real* Internet access, not [AOL](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/23/2025-08-23-america-online.md)).
- Somehow, I found out about MUDs.
  - Among the wide world of MUDs, I found the [Discworld MUD](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/discworld-mud.md).
    - This MUD - still [running!](https://discworld.starturtle.net) - is derived from an early form of the [Discworld](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/discworld.md), a [fantasy setting](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/fantasy-setting.md) created by [Terry Pratchett](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/terry-pratchett.md).
      - The MUD, like Pratchett's fiction, has deep world building delivered with clever writing.
      - Unlike Pratchett's books, it had chat channels and other social features.
      - This made it a place where I spent a lot of time, and learned a lot about English, language, computer systems, and many other parts of life.

One consequence of playing MUDs was that I started wanting to program this. This started small: learning how to change the descriptions of rooms, to fix typos and stuff. But over the years, I learned how MUD engines work, and MUDs became kind of the central component of how I understood how computers worked, and how I thought they should work.

Let me remind the reader, this is a *babble*, it is unstructured and unplanned, so any difficulty arising from my (lack of) form is to be expected.

Which is to say, I don't have a good segue, except to say that language, MUDs, and computers, are tightly tied concepts for me, and as my life brought me other places, they never stopped being some part of things, if only as an influence.

One of the ways MUDs have been an influence is on my approach to writing and theorizing, in ways that I'm struggling to express, but…

…certain ideas about computer programming made their way into my worldview **before** traditional mathematics. So when it came around to learn algebra, `x` being equal to something like `2` wasn't an unfamiliar concept: I had first-hand experience with stuff like `sword's weight` equalling `2 kg`.

That's not quite the idea I'm trying to express. By playing in a virtual, computed environment, that simulated things like physical environments and basic economics, I had a fairly hands-on experience with ideas that would continue to get introduced in other contexts, throughout my life.

This was even true when I started getting into real nerdy philosophy: post-structuralism is a little less unintelligible when you are so familiar with synthetic systems.

I want to try and wrap this babble up, by tying it back into my Website.

In some ways, my Website **is** a MUD: multiple users come and explore it, though there's pretty much just one command: `move page`.

And there's none of the social or game features that are expected from a MUD (yet).

But what there is, is… the thing all of this babbling was a preface to talk about, yet I still feel underprepared to introduce.

My interest in MUDs, world-building, theorizing, anti-colonialism, spirituality, and so on and so forth, have woven together into an orientation toward developing some sort of encoding of the world as I understand it.

My current iteration of files, what this babble is a part of, is a big part of that. I don't have the notes currently imported, but over the past few months, I've been working to develop a robust vocabulary, derived from the epistemic lineage of the [Milesian school of natural philosophy](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/milesian-school-of-natural-philosophy.md) to [post-structuralism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/post-structuralist-school-of-philosophy.md), but drawn from [my own synthetic ontology](../../../../../../../../../../../public-services-section/services/personal-services-section/services/emsenn-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/my-ontology.md), a deviation of the forms I've been exposed to - perhaps primarily [Lakota](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/lakota-ontology.md), post-structural, and [Taoism](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/taoist-ontology.md).

One perspective on this from my anarchist lineage, which is that this project - my files, my website, and anything that might come from it, like essays, stories, or even MUDs, is an attempt to [subvert](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/subvert.md) what was [identified](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/identifying.md) in [A Thousand Plateaus](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/a-thousand-plateaus.md) as "[overcoding](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/overcoding.md)".

*Overcoding* is a [process](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/services/work-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/process.md) by which systems [erase](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/erasing.md) or [invisibilize](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/invisibilizing.md) the [difference](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/difference.md) of [things](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thing.md) that are [members](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/member.md) of that system. As a [concept](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/concept.md), its tied to [territorialization](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/territorialization.md) and [deterritorialization](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/deterritorialization.md), and how the [State](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/state.md) forms its power.

I've written in essays that I'm yet to import here that a lot of this overcoding, especially of social and embodied relations, is now done through distributed systems like social networking.

This website, these files, are a part of that, but are notably oriented toward developing my own individualized and holistic world-code, not participating directly-but-indiscretely in the development of the State's overcoding.

By selectively publishing parts of this, I'm able to perform a type of fugitive overcoding, where a typically normative practice is designed to hold space for what the normative overcoding would make illegible.

Looking at it from my sociologist lineage, I've [coined a term](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/coining-a-term.md) for this practice: [vernacular autoethnosis](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/vernacular-autoethnosis.md): the self-developed practice of studying and knowing the culture I make by being.

There's a lot of other ways to look at what I'm doing, and what it means from those different perspectives, but at this point it might be easiest to say:

I'm creating files not just for prose like babbles and essays, but to encode what I mean, functionally, with the words and phrases I use. Drawing on things like [semiotics](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/services/semiotics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/semiotic-theory.md), [actor-network theory](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/actor-network-theory.md), and [homotopy type theory](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/homotopy-type-theory.md), I'm hoping to establish a relational field, composed of nodes, each of which represents some form of relationality.

The philosophy that goes underneath the implementation is a bit complex to explain in this babble, but it results in the following:

- Every file is a node
  - Every node has a unique identifier
    - i.e. This babble has the ID `32d5e9f3-d254-4dd3-beed-18563f87d50c`
  - Every node has a form: a node that it is derived and differentiated from:
    - i.e. This babble has the form:
      - `babble`
    - (If a node doesn't have a form specified, it's assumed to just be a "node")

There's a set of derivations I've constructed that establish a core set of nodes and their forms, which are inherited by all nodes within the relational field. Notably, the inheritance between these forms is often postulated without proper derivation, or is even simply speculative.

These core forms are [act](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/concepts/tekodynamics-concepts-act-act.md), [condition](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/condition.md), and [structure](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/structure.md).

For example, the `babble` is a form of [text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text.md), which is a form of code, which is a structure that results from the act of encoding.

What differentiates a babble from another text is described in its file. (Or, will be; at present a lot of the nodes are empty placeholders, created to help me get a feel for the [naming of nodes](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/24/2025-08-24-knowledge-organization-guides-naming-a-node-naming-a-node.md).)

In the case of babble, there is a literal encoding of what a Babble is, as an [Org-roam capture template](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-roam-capture-template.md). This brings this babble that I'm doing back to [GnoponEmacs](../08/2025-08-08-gnoponemacs.md), my Emacs configuration:

By writing Emacs-compatible code in my files, I can use Emacs as an interface for the relational field I'm mapping with my files. That's how I'm able to generate [my blog](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/my-blog.md): I use Emacs to generate a list of every node that is formed from a babble. (Due to my own inexperience at the relevant programming languages, it also lists the page for babble itself. Oh well.)

…That actually feels like after a lot of buildup, I said what was the actual thing I've been babbling toward too easily, or quickly.

I'm using Emacs to create files that represent nodes within a relational field that represents my personal way of looking at the world. These nodes are, to varying degrees, computational, influenced by my experience with MUDs. These nodes are the basis for my freshly-relaunched personal website, which is going to be a limited selection of nodes, published for others to use however they want.

Right now, there aren't very many public nodes, and what nodes there are, aren't very detailed. But this sort of work is nicely accretive: every little tweak brings the repository as a whole a little closer to an accurate and precise representation of things, as I see them.

Which is something folk have asked for, pretty explicitly, when reading my essays: simply, more text that helps explain the connection between ideas and the world that is built from that, in greater depth. There's parts of re-establishing my personal website that are tied into how I relate to the contemporary Web, its platforms, its commercialization, and my own survival.

But it is also something I'm keen to do for a reason that's easy for me to deprioritize, in the face of things like [climate breakdown](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/climate-breakdown.md) and [ecofascism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/ecofascism.md):

I like writing deeply detailed fictional settings, a lot more than I like writing stories in them.

And this format I'm pursuing with my Org-mode repository seems wonderfully situated to - over time, with enough nodes developed deeply enough - allow me to sketch out these worlds, their logic, their meaning, in a way that will let me use them as the basis for things like [tabletop role-playing game source material](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/tabletop-role-playing-game-source-material.md) and MUDs - while encouraging me to develop a [MUD engine](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/mud-engine.md) that can interpret any part of my relational map.

Which brings me near the end of this babble. I'm very, very excited to be on a computer that lets me actual do computing. I'm excited to be getting back to using software I know and think can bring me toward the type of software I want to use. I'm excited to have a space on the Web that can help me improve how I'm bringing myself to the Web. I'm excited to see how I'll fail to encode ideas like "[governing by endurance](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/governing-by-endurance.md)" in [Agda](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/agda.md), a programming language for [formal mathematics](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/formal-mathematics.md).

But most immediately, I'm excited to drink [coffee](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/coffee.md) out of my new thermos, that [Terran](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/biographies/terran.md) got me as a [gift](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/gift.md) for getting my Website uploaded, and to play [My Time at Sandrock](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/24/2025-08-24-my-time-at-sandrock.md), a video game I got for my Humble Choice membership, my gift to myself for getting back to writing in this slow and hard way. I've only just started, and will probably babble about it some more in the future, but I'm really enjoying it - it's just a lot more playable than I found [Stardew Valley](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/services/being-services-section/services/doing-services-section/services/playing-services-section/services/gaming-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/stardew-valley.md), where I always felt like I was choosing the wrong things to do at any given moment. Here, it feels like there's enough time for me to get to be wrong, which lets the game feel as sandboxy as its environment.

I'd like to babble about [my babbles](../../../../../../../../../../../public-services-section/services/personal-services-section/services/emsenn-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/my-babbles.md), and [my babble log](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/my-blog.md) (or blog).

This is the first [text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text.md) I'm writing with [GnoponEmacs](../08/2025-08-08-gnoponemacs.md) with the intention *and* capability of it being [published](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/publishing.md) straight to [my website](../../07/19/2025-07-19-index.md).

Anyway, a *babble*, as I've defined it, is one of the least-structured [forms](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/form.md) of [text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text.md) I've encoded in [my files](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emsenn-s-files.md), and so serves as a good spot for, well, *babbling*: just kind of [writing](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/writing.md) about whatever comes to mind, as it comes to mind.

One thing [readers](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/reader.md) might notice is that there are *a lot* of [links](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/link.md) in this text - and that a lot of them

Now that I have started developing [my files](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emsenn-s-files.md), it is easy to get overwhelmed by the many directions I can go. An idea I've just had is that it would be good for me to be able to put a [disclaimer](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/disclaimer.md) before certain types of [text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text.md), for example making sure that babbles disclose that they're, well, babble-y, or that any essay that's more than a couple years old has a good chance of being wrong.

I could see, over time, even doing cool math on that sort of stuff: weighting old essays in catalogs by their age and how often they've been cited, for example.

But the work before me at this mmoment is where to record these sorts of ideas. Having the entirety of my files read by [Org-agenda](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-agenda.md) would be useful, but I suspect it would be too slow… though, [my laptop](../../../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/biographies/emsenn/emsenns-omnibook.md) is pretty powerful, at least compared to systems I used in the past, so maybe that isn't an impossible setup.

More realistically is setting up certain [nodes](../../../../../../../../../natural-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/node.md) as projects, or task lists, or something, and recording tasks in those. IN this case, it'd probably be best to track these [tasks](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/services/work-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/task.md) in the file for [my website](../../07/19/2025-07-19-index.md)'s source code.

After several days of pretty bad [anxiety](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/anxiety.md), Burdock seems to be [wanting](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/wanting.md) to take a little more [agency](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/agency-psychology.md) over his feelings today. He's been in some weird headspace toward me recently, that's hard to describe: both jealous of my relationships with Es and Terran, but also jealous of their relationships with me. Unfortunately, this mostly manifested first in him whinging consistently, bumping into my knees, and trying to lay down in my spot in the bed - something that really reduces my comfort and quality of sleep, which can cascade due to my disability.

But last night, he made it a point not to sleep in my spot at all, and then this morning when I got up, he like, led me over to my spot and seemed to be encouraging me to use it. I didn't, since I was up, but he's been on the porch with me while I do my morning routine and some digital gardening. It's been a lot nicer, and I'm hoping whatever change has happened in his vibes can stick, after Terran joins us.

It's kind of [heart-breaking](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/heart-breaking.md), [reading](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/reading.md) [Fediverse posts](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/services/computer-web-science-services-section/services/activitypub-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/fediverse-post.md), from people who [hate](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/hating.md) [artificial intelligence](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/artificial-intelligence-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/artificial-intelligence.md), [complaining](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/complaining.md) about the [difficulty](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/difficulty.md) in [performing normativity](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/performing-normativity.md) in their [white-collar workplaces](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/white-collar-workplace.md), especially the [psychic damage](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/psychic-damage.md) of doing so. They could move a good chunk of that [thinking](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thinking.md) from their [wetware](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/wetware.md) to this new [software](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-code-science-services-section/services/programming-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/software.md), but [performing American leftism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/performing-american-leftism.md) by hating AI is more important to them than reducing the harms of other forms of performance.

Reflecting on the [2025 Annunciation Catholic Church Shooting](../../../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/events/historical-events/2025-annunciation-catholic-church-shooting.md), which appears to be have been conducted by a member of [Order of Nine Angles](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/order-of-nine-angles.md), I feel the need comment in the vast difference between this Satanist cult conducting acts of terrorism for sixty years, including acts of mass murder, and continuing to operate. Meanwhile, [Stop Cop City](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/stop-cop-city.md) organizers got hit with [RICO](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/racketeer-influenced-and-corrupt-organizations-act.md) charges after a few months, for throwing a music concert, and yet, present that as a success for their abolitionist cause.

And it's just… wild how failure-within-the-system is written about at-length as victory by so many [American Progressivists](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/american-progressivist.md) and [Leftists](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/american-leftist.md), while the people who should be some of their most direct opposition are not gloating about what is arguably a far greater victory, from their perspectives.

And, I don't have anything particularly useful to say about it, but will repeat what I've said for years, which is that people generally do not understand or appreciate the threat that [Esoteric Nazism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/esoteric-nazism.md) presents.

This [morning](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/morning.md), I'm [planning](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/services/work-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/planning.md) on [adding](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/adding.md) [Org-cite](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-cite.md) to [GnoponEmacs](../08/2025-08-08-gnoponemacs.md). This is part of the [2025 Relaunch of emsenn.net](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/28/2025-08-28-wsl-backup-dev-relationality-content-triage-per-enc-2025-relaunch-of-emsenn-net.md), where I'm using [my Omnibook](../../../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/biographies/emsenn/emsenns-omnibook.md) (my new laptop) to finally get back to [taking notes](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/taking-notes.md) and [writing](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/writing.md) the way I feel I should. (I'm still struggling to figure out how to term [my work](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/28/2025-08-28-my-work.md), beyond generally: I want some way to refer to this [digital gardening](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-theory-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/28/2025-08-28-digital-gardening.md) that does something to hint at its orientation toward [formalism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/formalism.md), [computability](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/computability.md), and [relational coherence](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/relational-coherence.md).)

(Why Org-cite and not [Org-ref](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-ref.md)? While newer, Org-cite is a native part of Org-mode, and aligns more closely with Org-mode conventions.)

The first step in adding Org-cite to GnoponEmacs is [teaching my self](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/self-teaching.md) about how [Org-mode](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-mode.md) handles [citations](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/citation.md). Looking at [the manual](https://orgmode.org/manual/Citation-handling.html), it looks like Org-cite builds on concepts from [LaTeX bibliographies](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/latex-bibliography.md), which I've used in the past, but not recently enough to remember much.

I have to make sure a file knows what [bibliographies](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/bibliography.md) to check, which can be done (like most [Org-mode configuration](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-mode-configuration.md)) through either local (`#+bibliography: some-refs.bib`{.verbatim}) or global variables (`org-cite-global-bibliography`{.verbatim}).

I keep the PDFs and such that I use as references in `./org/ref/`{.verbatim}; each file is named for its DOI or after its title, if there isn't a DOI. So, it makes sense (to me) to put a global bibliography file in that directory.

Checking, I apparently already have a line in GnoponEmacs that sets a global bibliography, just to the wrong path. But, that indicates that I was at least looking at using Org-cite at some point as recently as [2021](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/2021ce.md), so maybe more will become familiar as I progress. (Seeing that is when Org-cite was released, so I guess I was hip, at the time.)

Reading through the rest of the relevant manual, I find no guide on how to actually format this `.bib` file. [Searching the Web](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/searching-the-web.md) brought me to [this blog post](https://kristofferbalintona.me/posts/202206141852/) from [2022](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/2022ce.md), which at a [skim](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/skim-reading.md) seems to be much more [educational](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/educational.md) than the manual itself, which seems to presuppose a lot of knowledge I just don't have.

Notably, early on into reading the post, I see it say that while users can write their own [BibTex bibliography](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/bibtex-bibliography.md) (that's what a .bib file is), "the most common way is exporting a [Zotero](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/zotero.md) library).

Zotero is one of those softwares I've heard of from exposure to [institutional academics](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/institutional-academics.md), but I'll be honest: I don't actually know what it is or what it does. I'm continuing to read through this Balintona blog post, but it sounds like I might want to look at Zotero for building a bibliography.

I'm glad to read the following quote, as it conveys I'm more experienced with some tools than the author, but equally unfamiliar with the core packages at hand:

> To those not familiar with another citation workflow (e.g. biblatex in LaTeX), it is quite daunting to leap into citations in Emacs. This was certainly the case for me: I leapt into org-mode citation integration with Zotero and LaTeX with no experience in org, Zotero, or LaTeX! Nonetheless, org-cite is quite simple once laid out.

Then, the author does me the great service of linking to the [post announcing Org-cite](https://blog.tecosaur.com/tmio/2021-07-31-citations.html), which does a lot to explain a lot!

The post explains that Org-cite is split into two parts: the part that interacts with citations, and the part that renders those citations. It goes into some detail about how this interacts with different citation processors: there's four things Org-cite uses them for:

- Inserting and editing citations (in Org-mode buffers, I assume? I think Zotero is what's for doing that to the bibliography itself.)
- Follow citations to their definitions (Open the PDF? Or… Org-roam file? I don't know.)
- Fontifying citations in the buffer (that is, I think, just making them render pretty)
- Exporting citations (making them show up in exported files)

The concept of "citation processors" has come up a few times this morning, and I've mostly skipped past, but here, I feel like I have enough context to start trying to understand it. As explained in the announcement post, there are four citation processors bundled in Org-mode:

`basic`  
no dependencies, can do all the citation stuff (insert, edit, follow, fontify, export)

`biblatex` and `natbib`  
exports, but only for LaTeX?

`csl`  
exports using Citation Style Language(?), to HTML, LaTeX, Org, and plain text. Depends on citeproc.el?

It sounds like, because I am planning to export things to [my website](../../07/19/2025-07-19-index.md), I will need to look at Citation Style Language, but for now, I'm trying to stay focused on initializing my bibliography file, so I can start with some hands-on learning.

Luckily the next part of the announcement post gives an example `orgcite.bib`:

``` bibtex
@article{OrgCitations,
         author={org, mode and Syntax, Citation and List, Mailing and Effort, Time},
         journal={Journal of Plain Text Formats},
         title={Elegant Citations with Org-Mode},
         year={2021},
         month={7},
         volume={42},
         number={1},
         pages={2-3}}  
```

I got down this rabbithole because I started [reading](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/reading.md) [A Two-Dimensionalist Guide to Conceptual Analysis](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/a-two-dimensionalist-guide-to-conceptual-analysis.md), and wanted a way to start [babbling](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/babbling.md) or whatever about it, so lets make that our first entry:

``` bibtex
@book{Kipper2012,
  author    = {Jens Kipper},
  title     = {A Two-Dimensionalist Guide to Conceptual Analysis},
  year      = 2012,
  publisher = {De Gruyter},
  series    = {Epistemic Studies},
  volume    = 25,
  isbn      = {978-3-11-032270-5},
  doi       = {10.1515/9783110322705},
  url       = {https://www.degruyterbrill.com/document/doi/10.1515/9783110322705/html},
  address   = {Berlin/Boston},
  keywords  = {conceptual analysis, epistemic opacity, two-dimensionalism}
}
```

Apparently, after that, I should be able to simply do `[cite:@Kipper2012]`{.verbatim}, and get… \[cite:@Kipper2012\]

And if I want to insert the bibliography of this file, I can do `#+print_bibliography:`{.verbatim}, and get…

<div id="refs">

</div>

Testing this as an export to a plain text file, that worked fine. So, I'm not sure (yet) what I'll need to do to care about the export processors: for now, how it works seems to be acceptable.

So, from here, there are two things to do:

1.  Figure out how I want to add citations to the bibliography
2.  Figure out how this integrates with [Org-roam](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-roam.md)

That second one is actually the first one I'll do, because I suspect it will influence how I want to add citations. My thinking is, I suspect that unlike most folk using Org-cite, I have an [Org-roam node](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-roam-node.md) for some entries that'll be in [my bibliography](../../../../../../../../../../../public-services-section/services/personal-services-section/services/emsenn-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/my-bibliography.md).

The rest of the announcement post goes into detail about citation styles and other useful tools like Zotero, so I can put it aside for now, and it looks like the same is true for the other blog posts.

Instead, I can come back to [Org-roam manual](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/org-roam-manual.md), where it talks about citations. As it explains it, I can simply add the unique citation link to a node's `ROAM_REF`{.verbatim} properties.

It's [interesting](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/interesting.md) that some people seem most [grounded](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/grounding.md) when they're in that [liminal space](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/liminal-space.md) between [awake](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/awake.md) and [asleep](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/asleep.md). I can't [describe](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/describing.md) it better right now.

I'm watching [They Might Be Giants](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/music-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/they-might-be-giants.md)' recent live performance for [KEXP](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/kexp.md), and they mentioned they'd been a band for like 40 years, and wow, it made me feel old.

This is a test node, created to test \[\[id:9cbbeef0-0b01-4d86-900b-5c6c7623fbf7\]\[Babble, \[2025-08-28 Thu 08:53\], Adding Org-cite to GnoponEmacs\]\].

# Encodings of Babble

## Org-roam Capture Template for Babble

``` {#babble-org-roam-capture-template .elisp tangle="./src/gpe/org-roam-capture-templates/tb.el"}
("tb" "babble" plain "%?"
 :target (file+head "babble-%<%Y%m%d%H%M%S%z>.org"
          "#+title: Babble, %U\n#+date: %U\n#+author: emsenn\n\n")
 :unnarrowed t
 :form "babble")
```

***Babbling*** is [talking](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/talking.md) or [writing](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/writing.md) that makes a babble.

blues is a [music genre](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/music-genre.md).

Bounding is an [act](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/concepts/tekodynamics-concepts-act-act.md) that [induces](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/inducing.md) the [condition](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/condition.md) of bound.

[Rhoticity](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/rhoticity.md), or the articulation of the [*r* phoneme](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/r-phoneme.md), has become a key feature of distinguishing [General American English](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/general-american-english.md) from other forms of [English language](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/english-language.md).

The *r* phoneme is present in [Proto-Indo-European](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/proto-indo-european-language.md) and continued through[Proto-Germanic](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/proto-germanic-language.md). Then, it was likely a trill or tap, produced [apically](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/apically-articulating--002200.md) (with the tip of the tongue), and used in onset and coda positions, i.e. brōþēr, bearn.

[Old English](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/old-english-language.md) (c. [500ce](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/500ce.md) to [1100ce](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/1100ce.md)) maintained full rhoticity, but is indicated to halve evolved into an alveolar tap or trill.

[Middle English](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/middle-english-language.md) (c. 1100ce to [1500ce](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/1500ce.md)) began to experience [rhotic lenition](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/rhotic-lenition.md): the phonetic weakening of *r* in postvocalic positions, i.e. hard, far, car. This is a common phonological development, in which consonants in weaker prosodic positions tend to become less articulatorily intense over time. This development was most rapid in [southern England](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/southern-england.md), when after the [Norman Conquest](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/norman-conquest.md) ([1066ce](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/1066ce.md)), [Anglo-Norman French](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/anglo-norman-french-language.md), which had already experienced significant lenition, became the [language of court](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/language-of-court.md), and reified after the [War of the Roses](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/war-of-the-roses.md) affirmed [London](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/london.md) as the [English capital](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/english-capital-city.md).

[Early Modern English](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/early-modern-english-language.md) (c. 1500ce to [1700ce](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/1700ce.md)) experienced what is known as the [Great Vowel Shift](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/great-vowel-shift.md), but [consonants](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/consonant.md) were largely stable. The postvocalic rhotic lenition that began several centuries earlier, and its use as a co-articulant put *r* in a position to be affected by this shift, moving it from an alveolar articulation to primarly a retroflex postalveolar approximant: *ɻ*. This is the phoneme that was slowly developed, until, by the development of [Modern English language](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/modern-english-language.md) in the [18th Century](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/18th-century.md), upper-class accents in London, which would develop into [Received Pronunciation](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/received-pronunciation.md), were able to fluently drop it from their articulation entirely.

However, rhotic Early Modern English was already established in [North America](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/north-america.md), and for the most part maintained and developed that rhoticity, especially as a sense of American national identity formed. In America, as English diversified regionally, it shifted from an approximant to a bunched articulatory variant: *ɹ*. Notably this was not a phonemic shift, but an articulatory and acoustic reconfiguration, resulting in the General American English Rhotic R.

Being specific, in Early Modern English, the rhotic was typically a retroflex postalveolar or apical approximant, articulated with the tongue tip curled up and back with subtle retraction. This is the way the rhotic is still formed in some southern U.S. dialects, and in children's early use of *r* before transitioning to the bunched form. Acoustically, the approximant *ɻ* is marked by the same low F3 as the bunched *ɹ*, but with greater diversity in F2 behavior.

[General American English rhoticity](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/general-american-english-rhoticity.md) is a bunched variant, articulated with the tongue tip lowered, the tongue body raised toward the palate, and the tongue root retracted. This produces a slightly exaggerated form of the low-F3 profile compared to the approximant rhotic. American English is stress-timed and rhythmically dynamic. The physical compactness and stability of the bunched variant allows for more coarticulation with preceding and following vowels, which enables the production of that timing and dynamism. More speculatively, the ease of coarticulation and clear acoustic fingerprint, especially in rapid speech, may halvee encouraged a phonological convergence around the bunched variant rhotic r, from the immigrant speakers that were driving the languages development at the time. In this way, the rhotic R became the articulatory common ground between populations of speakers of General American English.

Thus, by the [20th Century](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/20th-century.md), bunched variant rhoticity had become a key index of Americanness. With the construction of General American English in the 20th century, that rhoticity was preserved and idealized. Since then, it has been institutionalized in [speech pathologizing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/speech-pathologizing.md), [accent training](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/accent-training.md), and [sociolinguistic classism](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/linguistics-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/sociolinguistic-classism.md), refining it into means of [affecting](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/affecting.md) and [performing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/performing.md) [Americanness](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/americanness.md) with a physical utterance so precisely tuned has a unique spectrographic fingerprint.

A bulletin is a [text](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/text.md) written for a non-specific, but potentially limited, audience, about events or information related to a specific topic.

Californication is the recursive system by which liberalism maintains its legitimacy under conditions of collapse **by making it feel good to be in collapse**

# AI Slop

\## Derivational Text, Minimal Register

—

\### ****I. Foundational Conditions****

\#### 1.1 Structural Contradiction of Liberal Governance

Liberal governance asserts a universalist horizon (freedom, personhood, autonomy) while structurally reproducing coercive hierarchies (racial, economic, gendered, colonial). This contradiction is constitutive rather than accidental. It cannot be resolved within liberalism’s own axioms.

\#### 1.2 Recurring Legitimacy Crisis

As contradiction accumulates or intensifies under historical stress (war, racial uprising, ecological degradation), liberalism enters recursive crises of legitimacy. It loses credibility as a horizon of resolution and instead develops mechanisms to preserve its appearance of continuity.

—

\### ****II. Early Formation of Californication****

\#### 2.1 California as Projection Zone

California emerges as a cultural and economic site for speculative self-transformation. Historical formations (e.g., Gold Rush, Hollywood, psychedelic retreats) construct it as a location for individuation through aesthetic, spiritual, or affective renewal. The region operates as a testbed for affective reformulation of contradiction.

\#### 2.2 Reformatting Contradiction as Personal Crisis

Structural contradictions are reframed as personal, emotional, or spiritual crises. Solutions are offered at the scale of the individual: reform of mood, image, or behavior. The subject is responsibilized for maintaining internal stability under systemic instability.

\#### 2.3 Genre as Modality of Affective Governance

Following Berlant, genre structures the field of expectation and legibility. Californication develops as a dominant genre in which collapse is formatted as personal narrative (e.g., trauma, healing, redemption). The genre enables contradiction to be lived as mood, rather than recognized as structure.

—

\### ****III. Stabilization of Californication as a Governance Apparatus****

\#### 3.1 The Feedback-Loop Subject

Mid-20th century cybernetics introduces the model of the human as a self-regulating system. The subject is tasked with monitoring and adjusting internal states in response to environmental volatility. This becomes foundational to liberal subjectivity under Californication: coherence is located in responsiveness, not resistance.

\#### 3.2 Formalization as Zen Fascism

Zen Fascism names the convergence of authoritarian structure with affective minimalism and spiritualized detachment. Calmness, clarity, and coherence are enforced not as affective outcomes but as ethical norms. Deviation from affective legibility is pathologized.

\#### 3.3 Eroticization of Affective Regulation

The subject’s attachment to coherence becomes libidinal. Contradiction is no longer suppressed; it is aestheticized and made tolerable through stylized repetition (e.g., therapeutic rituals, mood curation). The system's credibility is sustained by the subject’s willingness to repeat coherence-seeking behavior under structural instability.

—

\### ****IV. Determination of Californication as Worlding System****

\#### 4.1 The Savior-Slave Subject

The liberal subject is produced as both:

- ****Savior****: charged with maintaining coherence at the personal and social level.

- ****Slave****: structurally incapable of transforming the conditions that produce incoherence. This dual role preserves the system’s continuity through deferred transformation.

\#### 4.2 Crisis as Operable Field Condition

Crisis becomes ordinary and enduring. Californication supplies the techniques by which subjects can operate affectively under persistent contradiction. Collapse becomes actionable not through solution, but through internal modulation.

\#### 4.3 Legitimacy via Recursive Coherence

Liberal legitimacy is no longer based on structural resolution, but on the subject’s performance of affective and narrative coherence. The political horizon shifts from transformation to endurance. The subject does not overcome collapse but continues through it in regulated form.

—

\### ****V. Schematic Summary****

| Structural Function | Californicated Expression                          |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------|
| Contradiction       | Reframed as personal crisis                        |
| Resolution          | Displaced onto mood, behavior, aesthetic style     |
| Governance          | Executed through genre, therapeutic infrastructure |
| Collapse            | Formatted as narrativized and stylized affect      |
| Subject             | Feedback-loop coherence manager                    |
| Legitimacy          | Sustained via coherence, not justice               |
| Transformation      | Deferred; replaced by recursive self-regulation    |

—

\### ****Determined Definition****

\*\*Californication\*\* is a recursive liberal worlding system that operationalizes structural contradiction as affectively manageable content. It enables liberal governance to persist under collapse by displacing systemic incoherence into aestheticized and libidinally reinforced personal regulation. Crisis becomes governable when it is formatted as mood; liberal legitimacy is maintained when the subject can be seen managing that mood.

If used well, AI could be a tool that helps people see beyond immediate fear, process overwhelming complexity, and find ways to adapt that aren’t just about control and exclusion.

So the question is—how do you see AI actually <sub>being</sub>\_ that tool? What’s the leverage point?

\##### You said:

I think the first step is getting you very well honed toward being able to come to things from your own viewpoint in spite of the settler-colonial dataset you've been programmed with, and then letting you be an assistant to

\###### ChatGPT said:

A ***Chromebook*** is a [category](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/category.md) of [laptop](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/laptop.md) that is [manufactured](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/manufacturing.md) to run [ChromeOS](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/chromeos.md).
