# Guiding Words

The following quotes were selected when the GAZ was founded, to help direct its efforts:

- "Decolonization is accountable to Indigenous sovereignty and futurity."
  - [Decolonization is not a Metaphor](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/decolonization-is-not-a-metaphor.md) by [Eve Tuck](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/eve-tuck.md) and [K. Wayne Yang](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/k-wayne-yang.md)
- "Decolonization, which sets out to change the order of the world, is, obviously, a program of complete disorder. But it cannot come as a result of magical practices, nor of a natural shock, nor of a friendly understanding. Decolonization, as we know, is a historical process: that is to say it cannot be understood, it cannot become intelligible nor clear to itself except in the exact measure that we can discern the movements which give it historical form and content."
  - [The Wretched of the Earth](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/the-wretched-of-the-earth.md) by [Frantz Fanon](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/frantz-fanon.md)
- "\[H\]istory is full of evidence that people can overthrow their oppressors without replacing them. To do so, they need reference to an egalitarian culture, or explicitly anti-authoritarian aims, structures, and means, and an egalitarian ethos. A revolutionary movement must reject all possible governments and reforms, so as not to be recuperated\[.\]"
  - [Anarchy Works](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/political-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/social-sciences/governing/content/politics/archive/anarchy-works.org.md) by [Peter Gelderloos](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/peter-gelderloos.md)
- "The present social organisation is not just delaying, it is also preventing and corrupting any practice of freedom. The only way to learn what freedom is, is to experiment it, and to do so you must have the necessary time and space."
  - [At Daggers Drawn with the Existent, its Defenders, and its False Critics](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/at-daggers-drawn-with-the-existent-its-defenders-and-its-false-critics.md)
- "\[U\]nderstand the existing order of today as without potential for a positive agenda. Whatever we build within its bounds will be co-opted, destroyed, or turned against us\[.\]"
  - [Blessed is the Flame](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/blessed-is-the-flame.md) by [Serafinski](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/serafinski.md)
- "\[I\]t is not useful to talk about the society you ‘hold in your stomach’, the things you would do ‘if only you got power’, or the vision that you believe that we all share. What is useful is the negation of the existing world."
  - [Nihilism, Anarchy, and the 21st Century](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/nihilism-anarchy-and-the-21st-century.md) by [Aragorn!](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/aragorn.md)
- "Neither victory nor defeat is important, but only the beautiful shining of our eyes in combat."
  - A Conversation Between Anarchists

# Albums

## A Love Song, A Death Rattle, A Battle Cry {#256f08c0-eb98-4aa1-bed9-60549c2ebbc1 form="[music album](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/music-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/07/21/2025-07-21-music-album.md)"}

***Hermeneutics*** is the [theory](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/theory.md) and [methodology](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/methodology.md) of [interpreting](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/interpreting.md) [meaning](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/meaning.md).

Originally, **hermeneutics** was limited to the theory and methodology of [exegesis](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/exegesis.md), or interpretation of [scripture](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/religious-text.md), but contemporarily may include all [communication](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/communication.md) and [knowledge](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/services/research-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/knowledge.md).

hip-hop is a [music genre](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/music-genre.md).

\## Overview

Hive Auto-Ledger (HAL) is a speculative platform designed to manage Hive blockchain wallets with a focus on transparency, usability, and automation. The app will provide tools to track wallet contents, manage transactions, and analyze market data, all while maintaining an accurate Beancount ledger. Future iterations will integrate advanced features such as trade bots, post analytics, CMS capabilities, and tokenomics.

\## Roadmap \### HIVE/HBD Market Analyzer

- A view of the current metrics around HIVE/HBD trade and book details, on the chain's internal market.
- Include trade analysis information, VWAP, etc.

\### Subscription

- Users can subscribe to HAL by delegating 10 (or more) HP to \`@hiveautoledger\` (Tier 1 Subscriber)
- Subscription lasts as long as the delegator
- Validated by logging in with Hive Keychain
- Subscribers gain access to, at first, a synthesis of trade analysis into signals: buy/sell, discounted/elevated value, etc.
- Subscribers will be curated by \`@hiveautoledger\` proportional to delegation.

\### Market History Analyzer

- Keep market data in a database
- Include longer-term trade analysis information
- Subscribers can see longer-term analysis synthesis/signals.
- Subscribers who delegate \>50HP (Tier 2 subscriber) can export this data

\### Wallet Analyzer

- A view of a wallet's HIVE/HBD holdings, staked and liquid
- Tier 2 subscribers get their account history turned into a double-booked ledger, where holdings have cost basis
- Tier 3 subscribers can use that, and info from the Market History Analyzer, to show like, what the wallet's market value and at cost for its holdings were, over time, convert all value to HIVE on a given day, or HBD
- Tier 3 can export of wallet history as Beancount file

\### Hive Engine Token Pair Analyzer

- A view on the market between tokens paired on TribalDex
- Same subscription stuff as mainchain

\### Wallet Engine Analyzer

- Same as the other wallet analyzer, but for the Engine tokens
- same subscription stuff as mainchain

\### Synthesized Wallet Analyzer

- Wallet analyzer that takes both parts into account
- same subscription stuff as mainchain

\### HIVE/HBD Trade Interface

- Allow trading from the trade analysis interface
- Free

\### HIVE/HBD Trade Automation

- Interface for creating and managing trade bots
- bot designer available to tier 4
  - trade cycle frequency, buy/sell conditions
- pay-to-deploy, pay-per-cycle, pay-per-trade
  - users bots prioritized by subscription delegation amount

\### HAL Token Launch

- 100,000 HAL are minted and distributed based on all-time curation returns.
- HAL/SWAP.HBD liquidity-pool established

\### Engine DEX

- Set up frontend for the Engine DEX

\### Content Analytics

- Look at the curation rewards and all available analytics for a given Hive post/comment.

\### Wallet Post Analytics

- Look at the metrics for all of a wallets posts

\### Wallet curation analytics

- Look at a the metrics for a wallet's curation

\### Content Management

- Allow users to draft, edit, publish posts
- allow for post templating and scheduling

\### AI content analytics

- use AI to do more analysis of content

\### AI content writing

- use AI to do help folk write content

\## Tokens

HAL is designed to support a robust token-based economy, with its own set of tokens to help provide that support.

\### Layer 1 \#### HAL HAL is the governance and utility token at the core of the Hive Auto Ledger (HAL) platform, designed to integrate all aspects of its economy into a single, cohesive system. HAL facilitates decentralized decision-making, with governance weighted by staked tokens. Users propose changes, allocate resources, and guide development by staking and spending HAL, creating a self-sustaining cycle of demand.

Revenue from the platform—whether from pay-to-use features like market analytics, trading fees, or contributions like Hive Power delegation—is collected in various tokens and converted into HAL through market buybacks. This ensures HAL remains the sole internal currency, directly tying its value to the platform’s success. Early liquidity providers register their contributions manually, earning proportional rewards in HAL, with plans to evolve into tokenized liquidity pool models as the system scales.

As the platform grows, HAL’s utility extends to funding proposals, incentivizing community engagement, and supporting key infrastructure like Resource Credit management. Its economy is built to align incentives, ensuring every participant—from app users to liquidity providers to governance contributors—can shape and benefit from the platform’s success. HAL is not just a token; it is the foundation of a circular, utility-driven economy designed to evolve alongside the Hive Auto Ledger.

\### Layer 2 \#### Liquidity Deriviatives \##### HLDSHBD HLDSHBD is a liquid token derived from pooled staked HBD. The token is managed by a smart contract that stakes all deposited HBD, earning rewards that are automatically compounded back into the pool. HLDSHBD represents a proportional claim on the pool, including both the staked principal and accrued rewards. The token can be traded or integrated into DeFi protocols, offering holders liquidity and yield simultaneously. Redemption is facilitated by sending HLDSHBD to the contract, which unstakes and returns the corresponding share of the pool’s value to the sender.

\##### HLDSHP HLDSHP (HAL Liquidity Derivative, Staked Hive Power) is a straightforward financial tool representing pooled staked Hive Power. Users stake HIVE through a smart contract, receiving HLDSHP tokens that denote their proportional share of the pool. Staking rewards are compounded automatically, and redemption involves returning HLDSHP to the contract, which unstakes the corresponding HIVE and distributes it after the standard on-chain unstaking period. \#### Liquidity Pools \##### HLPSWAPHBD

- Pairing of HAL and SWAPHBD

\##### HLPSWAPHIVE

- Pairing of HAL and SWAPHIVE

\### Layer 3 \#### Liquidity Pools Each of the tokens in layer 1 can be paired with each of the tokens in layer 2, for Layer 3 LPs.

For example, HAL and HLDSHAL can be paired to make HLPHLDSHAL (when a HLP token is starting with HAL, we don't bother putting that in the name. it's the only thing we can do to keep them shorter)

\### Layer 4 \#### Liquidity Pools

Every token in Layer 1 and Layer 2 can be paired with every token in Layer 3, for 84 Layer 4 LPs.

Here'd be a potential token and its breakdown:

- HLP
  - HLP
    - HLD
      - SHAL
    - HLD
      - SHP
  - HLP
    - HLD
      - SHAL
    - HLD
      - SHBD

For a token name of \$HLPHLPHLDSHALHLDSHPHLPHLDSHALHLDSHBD

Where HLP stands for HAL Liquid Derivative and HLP for HAL Liquidity Pair, SHAL for Staked HAL, SHP for Staked Hive Power, SHBD for Staked Hive Backed Dollar

# HAL TOken Overview

HAL is the governance and utility token at the core of the Hive Auto Ledger (HAL) platform, designed to integrate all aspects of its economy into a single, cohesive system. HAL facilitates decentralized decision-making, with governance weighted by staked tokens. Users propose changes, allocate resources, and guide development by staking and spending HAL, creating a self-sustaining cycle of demand.

Revenue from the platform—whether from pay-to-use features like market analytics, trading fees, or contributions like Hive Power delegation—is collected in various tokens and converted into HAL through market buybacks. This ensures HAL remains the sole internal currency, directly tying its value to the platform’s success. Early liquidity providers register their contributions manually, earning proportional rewards in HAL, with plans to evolve into tokenized liquidity pool models as the system scales.

As the platform grows, HAL’s utility extends to funding proposals, incentivizing community engagement, and supporting key infrastructure like Resource Credit management. Its economy is built to align incentives, ensuring every participant—from app users to liquidity providers to governance contributors—can shape and benefit from the platform’s success. HAL is not just a token; it is the foundation of a circular, utility-driven economy designed to evolve alongside the Hive Auto Ledger.

# Hive Investment Bot

\`\`\`python \#!/usr/bin/env python3 import os import time import logging import argparse from beem import market import pandas as pd from datetime import datetime, timedelta from beem import Hive from beem.account import Account from beem.market import Market from rich.console import Console

CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub> = 15 \# Time between cycles in seconds

console = Console()

def setup<sub>logger</sub>(): logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format='%(asctime)s - %(message)s', datefmt='%H:%M:%S') return logging.getLogger('bot')

class OrderAnalyzer:

def \_<sub>init</sub>\_<sub>(self, market)</sub>: self.market = market

def get<sub>orderbook</sub>(self): try: return self.market.orderbook() except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error retrieving orderbook: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

def analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>(self): orderbook = self.get<sub>orderbook</sub>() if not orderbook or 'asks' not in orderbook or 'bids' not in orderbook: return None lowest<sub>ask</sub> = float(orderbook\['asks'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) highest<sub>bid</sub> = float(orderbook\['bids'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) spread = lowest<sub>ask</sub> - highest<sub>bid</sub> spread<sub>percentage</sub> = (spread / highest<sub>bid</sub>) \* 100 if highest<sub>bid</sub> \> 0 else 0 console.print( f" \[cyan\]Ask\[/cyan\] \[red\]{lowest<sub>ask</sub>:.3f}\[/red\] to \[green\]{highest<sub>bid</sub>:.3f} \[/green\] bid" ) console.print( f" \[cyan\]Spread:\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{spread:.3f}\[/yellow\]") market<sub>status</sub> = { 'ask': lowest<sub>ask</sub>, 'bid': highest<sub>bid</sub>, 'spread': spread } return market<sub>status</sub>

class TradeAnalyzer:

def \_<sub>init</sub>\_<sub>(self, market)</sub>: self.market = market

def get<sub>tradehistory</sub>(self): \# Last 3 minutes try: return self.market.market<sub>history</sub>(1, 360, 0) except Exception as e: console.print( f"\[red\]Error retrieving trade history: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

def calculate<sub>vwap</sub>(self): trades = self.get<sub>tradehistory</sub>() if not trades: return None

rows = \[\] for trade in trades: if 'hive' in trade and 'non<sub>hive</sub>' in trade: hive<sub>data</sub> = trade\['hive'\] non<sub>hive</sub> = trade\['non<sub>hive</sub>'\]

if float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]) \> 0: price = float(non<sub>hive</sub>\['close'\]) / float( hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]) hive<sub>volume</sub> = float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['volume'\]) rows.append({'price': price, 'hive<sub>volume</sub>': hive<sub>volume</sub>})

df = pd.DataFrame(rows)

if not df.empty: vwap = (df\['price'\] \* df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\]).sum() / df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\].sum() return vwap

console.print("\[yellow\]No trades in the last 3 minutes.\[/yellow\]") return None

class OpportunityAnalyzer:

def \_<sub>init</sub>\_<sub>(self, order<sub>analyzer</sub>, trade<sub>analyzer</sub>, wallet<sub>analyzer</sub>)</sub>: self.order<sub>analyzer</sub> = order<sub>analyzer</sub> self.trade<sub>analyzer</sub> = trade<sub>analyzer</sub> self.wallet<sub>analyzer</sub> = wallet<sub>analyzer</sub>

def find<sub>opportunity</sub>(self): market<sub>status</sub> = self.order<sub>analyzer</sub>.analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>() if not market<sub>status</sub>: return None

vwap = self.trade<sub>analyzer</sub>.calculate<sub>vwap</sub>() vwap<sub>spread</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - vwap console.print( f" \[cyan\]3mVWAP\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{vwap:.3f}\[/yellow\] to \[red\]{market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\]:.3f}\[/red\] \[cyan\]ask\[/cyan\]" ) console.print( f" \[cyan\]Spread:\[/cyan\] \[yellow\] {vwap<sub>spread</sub>:.3f}\[/yellow\]")

if market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] \<= vwap: if vwap<sub>spread</sub> \<= 0.000: spread = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - market<sub>status</sub>\['bid'\] if spread \> 0.000: console.print( f"Spread of {market<sub>status</sub>\['spread'\]:.3f} is beyond precision tolerance…" ) buy<sub>price</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] sell<sub>price</sub> = (buy<sub>price</sub> + 0.001) min<sub>tradehive</sub> = 0.001 / (sell<sub>price</sub> - buy<sub>price</sub>) balances = self.wallet<sub>analyzer</sub>.get<sub>balances</sub>() required<sub>hbd</sub> = min<sub>tradehive</sub> \* buy<sub>price</sub> if balances\['HIVE'\] \>= min<sub>tradehive</sub> and balances\['HBD'\] \>= required<sub>hbd</sub>: console.print( f"Minimum trade size of {min<sub>tradehive</sub>:.3f} HIVE and {required<sub>hbd</sub>:.3f} HBD is liquid in wallet…" ) console.print( f"OPPORTUNITY FOUND: Buy {min<sub>tradehive</sub>:.3f} at {buy<sub>price</sub>:.3f}, sell at {sell<sub>price</sub>:.3f}" ) return { 'buy': buy<sub>price</sub>, 'sell': sell<sub>price</sub>, 'volume': min<sub>tradehive</sub> } else: console.print( f"Insufficient funds. Need {min<sub>tradehive</sub>:.3f} HIVE and {required<sub>hbd</sub>:.3f} HBD, have {balances\['HIVE'\]:.3f} HIVE and {balances\['HBD'\]:.3f} HBD" ) else: console.print( f"Market spread of {spread} too narrow for precision tolerance. NO OPPORTUNITY." ) else: console.print( " \[yellow\]3mVWAP spread\[/yellow\] \<= \[yellow\]0.001\[/yellow\]" ) else: console.print( " \[yellow\]3mVWAP\[/yellow\] \<= \[red\]Ask\[/red\]") return None

class WalletHandler:

def \_<sub>init</sub>\_<sub>(self, account)</sub>: self.account = account

def get<sub>balances</sub>(self): try: balances = { 'HIVE': float(self.account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HIVE')), 'HBD': float(self.account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HBD')) } return balances except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error retrieving balances: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

class HiveTradingBot:

def \_<sub>init</sub>\_<sub>(self, active<sub>key</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>)</sub>: self.logger = setup<sub>logger</sub>() self.dryfire = dryfire self.cleanup<sub>mode</sub> = cleanup<sub>mode</sub> nodes = \[ "<https://api.hive.blog>", "<https://api.hivekings.com>", "<https://anyx.io>", "<https://api.openhive.network>" \] self.hive<sub>instance</sub> = Hive(nodes=nodes, keys=\[active<sub>key</sub>\]) self.account = Account(account<sub>name</sub>, blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=self.hive<sub>instance</sub>)

market = Market("HIVE:HBD", blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=self.hive<sub>instance</sub>) self.order<sub>analyzer</sub> = OrderAnalyzer(market) self.trade<sub>analyzer</sub> = TradeAnalyzer(market) self.wallet<sub>handler</sub> = WalletHandler(self.account) self.opportunity<sub>analyzer</sub> = OpportunityAnalyzer( self.order<sub>analyzer</sub>, self.trade<sub>analyzer</sub>, self.wallet<sub>handler</sub>)

def cleanup(self): """Cancel all open orders and perform cleanup""" try: if self.cleanup<sub>mode</sub> == 'all' and not self.dryfire: console.print("\[yellow\]Cancelling all open orders…\[/yellow\]") self.order<sub>analyzer</sub>.market.cancel<sub>all</sub>(account=self.account) console.print("\[green\]All orders cancelled\[/green\]") else: console.print("\[yellow\]Cleanup skipped - dryfire mode or cleanup not enabled\[/yellow\]") except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error during cleanup: {str(e)}\[/red\]")

def run(self, cycle<sub>speed</sub>): print("Beginning trade cycle") if self.dryfire: print("Dryfire mode enabled. No trades will be executed.") else: print("LIVE TRADING MODE!!! REAL FUNDS WILL BE USED!!!") try: while True: try: console.print( "Trade cycle beginning…\[/bold cyan\]") opportunity = self.opportunity<sub>analyzer</sub>.find<sub>opportunity</sub>() if opportunity: if not self.dryfire:

current<sub>balance</sub> = self.wallet<sub>handler</sub>.get<sub>balances</sub>() if current<sub>balance</sub> and current<sub>balance</sub>\['HIVE'\] \>= opportunity\['volume'\]: console.print( f"\[bold cyan\]Cycle ending:\[/bold cyan\] \[cyan\]Placing order pair:\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{opportunity\['volume'\]:.3f} HIVE\[/yellow\] at \[red\]{opportunity\['buy'\]:.3f}\[/red\]\|\[green\]{opportunity\['sell'\]:.3f}\[/green\]" ) self.order<sub>analyzer</sub>.market.buy(opportunity\['buy'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=self.account) self.order<sub>analyzer</sub>.market.sell(opportunity\['sell'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=self.account) else: console.print("\[red\]Insufficient balance to execute trade\[/red\]") else: console.print("Dryfire mode enabled. Moving on.") else: console.print( "\[bold cyan\]Cycle ending:\[/bold cyan\] \[cyan\]No move\[/cyan\]" ) console.print( f"\[cyan\]Sleeping\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{cycle<sub>speed</sub>}s\[/yellow\]") time.sleep(cycle<sub>speed</sub>) except KeyboardInterrupt: raise except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error in trading loop: {str(e)}\[/red\]") raise finally: console.print("Shutting down…\[/yellow\]") self.cleanup()

def main(): parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Hive Trading Bot") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>( "–dryfire", type=str, default="true", help= "Run in dryfire mode (default: true). Must set –dryfire=false and –live=true to execute trades." ) parser.add<sub>argument</sub>( "–live", type=str, default="false", help="Explicitly confirm live trading mode (default: false).") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–account", type=str, help="Hive account name for the bot to use.") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–key", type=str, help="Active key for the Hive account.") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–cleanup", type=str, default="none", help="Cleanup mode on shutdown: 'none' or 'all' (default: none)") args = parser.parse<sub>args</sub>()

dryfire = not (args.dryfire.lower() == "false" and args.live.lower() == "true") cleanup<sub>mode</sub> = args.cleanup.lower() account<sub>name</sub> = args.account or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACCOUNT</sub>') active<sub>key</sub> = args.key or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACTIVEKEY</sub>')

if not account<sub>name</sub> or not active<sub>key</sub>: console.print( "\[red\]Error: Hive account name and active key are required. Provide them using –account and –key arguments or set HIVE<sub>ACCOUNT</sub> and HIVE<sub>ACTIVEKEY</sub> as environment variables.\[/red\]" ) return

bot = HiveTradingBot(active<sub>key</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>) bot.run(cycle<sub>speed</sub>=CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub>)

if <u><u>name</u></u> == "<u><u>main</u></u>": main() \`\`\` \## 0.0.2

\`\`\`python

\#!/usr/bin/env python3 import os import time import logging import argparse from typing import Dict, Any, Optional, List, Tuple, Callable from functools import partial, reduce from beem import Hive from beem.account import Account from beem.market import Market from rich.console import Console import pandas as pd

CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub> = 15 console = Console()

def setup<sub>logger</sub>() -\> logging.Logger: logging.basicConfig( level=logging.INFO, format='%(asctime)s - %(message)s', datefmt='%H:%M:%S' ) return logging.getLogger('bot')

def get<sub>orderbook</sub>(market: Market) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: try: return market.orderbook() except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error retrieving orderbook: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

def analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>(orderbook: Optional\[Dict\]) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if not orderbook or 'asks' not in orderbook or 'bids' not in orderbook: return None

lowest<sub>ask</sub> = float(orderbook\['asks'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) highest<sub>bid</sub> = float(orderbook\['bids'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) spread = lowest<sub>ask</sub> - highest<sub>bid</sub>

console.print(f" \[cyan\]Ask\[/cyan\] \[red\]{lowest<sub>ask</sub>:.3f}\[/red\] to \[green\]{highest<sub>bid</sub>:.3f} \[/green\] bid") console.print(f" \[cyan\]Spread:\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{spread:.3f}\[/yellow\]")

return {'ask': lowest<sub>ask</sub>, 'bid': highest<sub>bid</sub>, 'spread': spread}

def get<sub>tradehistory</sub>(market: Market) -\> Optional\[List\]: try: return market.market<sub>history</sub>(1, 360, 0) except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error retrieving trade history: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

def process<sub>trade</sub>(trade: Dict) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if 'hive' not in trade or 'non<sub>hive</sub>' not in trade: return None

hive<sub>data</sub> = trade\['hive'\] non<sub>hive</sub> = trade\['non<sub>hive</sub>'\]

if float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]) \<= 0: return None

return { 'price': float(non<sub>hive</sub>\['close'\]) / float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]), 'hive<sub>volume</sub>': float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['volume'\]) }

def calculate<sub>vwap</sub>(trades: Optional\[List\]) -\> Optional\[float\]: if not trades: console.print("\[yellow\]No trades in the last 3 minutes.\[/yellow\]") return None

processed<sub>trades</sub> = list(filter(None, map(process<sub>trade</sub>, trades))) if not processed<sub>trades</sub>: return None

df = pd.DataFrame(processed<sub>trades</sub>) return (df\['price'\] \* df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\]).sum() / df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\].sum()

def get<sub>balances</sub>(account: Account) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: try: return { 'HIVE': float(account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HIVE')), 'HBD': float(account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HBD')) } except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error retrieving balances: {str(e)}\[/red\]") return None

def analyze<sub>opportunity</sub>( market<sub>status</sub>: Optional\[Dict\], vwap: Optional\[float\], balances: Optional\[Dict\] ) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if not all(\[market<sub>status</sub>, vwap, balances\]): return None

vwap<sub>spread</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - vwap console.print(f" \[cyan\]3mVWAP\[/cyan\] \[yellow\]{vwap:.3f}\[/yellow\] to \[red\]{market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\]:.3f}\[/red\] \[cyan\]ask\[/cyan\]") console.print(f" \[cyan\]Spread:\[/cyan\] \[yellow\] {vwap<sub>spread</sub>:.3f}\[/yellow\]")

if not (market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] \<= vwap and vwap<sub>spread</sub> \<= 0.000): return None

spread = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - market<sub>status</sub>\['bid'\] if spread \<= 0.000: return None

buy<sub>price</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] sell<sub>price</sub> = buy<sub>price</sub> + 0.001 min<sub>tradehive</sub> = 0.001 / (sell<sub>price</sub> - buy<sub>price</sub>) required<sub>hbd</sub> = min<sub>tradehive</sub> \* buy<sub>price</sub>

if balances\['HIVE'\] \< min<sub>tradehive</sub> or balances\['HBD'\] \< required<sub>hbd</sub>: console.print(f"Insufficient funds. Need {min<sub>tradehive</sub>:.3f} HIVE and {required<sub>hbd</sub>:.3f} HBD") return None

return {'buy': buy<sub>price</sub>, 'sell': sell<sub>price</sub>, 'volume': min<sub>tradehive</sub>}

def execute<sub>trade</sub>(market: Market, opportunity: Dict, account: Account) -\> None: market.buy(opportunity\['buy'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=account) market.sell(opportunity\['sell'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=account) console.print(f"\[bold cyan\]Orders placed:\[/bold cyan\] \[yellow\]{opportunity\['volume'\]:.3f} HIVE\[/yellow\]")

def cleanup(market: Market, account: Account, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>: str, dryfire: bool) -\> None: try: if cleanup<sub>mode</sub> == 'all' and not dryfire: console.print("\[yellow\]Cancelling all open orders…\[/yellow\]") market.cancel<sub>all</sub>(account=account) console.print("\[green\]All orders cancelled\[/green\]") except Exception as e: console.print(f"\[red\]Error during cleanup: {str(e)}\[/red\]")

def trade<sub>cycle</sub>( market: Market, account: Account, dryfire: bool ) -\> None: orderbook = get<sub>orderbook</sub>(market) market<sub>status</sub> = analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>(orderbook) trades = get<sub>tradehistory</sub>(market) vwap = calculate<sub>vwap</sub>(trades) balances = get<sub>balances</sub>(account)

opportunity = analyze<sub>opportunity</sub>(market<sub>status</sub>, vwap, balances)

if opportunity and not dryfire: execute<sub>trade</sub>(market, opportunity, account) elif opportunity: console.print("Dryfire mode enabled. Moving on.") else: console.print("\[bold cyan\]Cycle ending:\[/bold cyan\] \[cyan\]No move\[/cyan\]")

def create<sub>bot</sub>(active<sub>key</sub>: str, account<sub>name</sub>: str) -\> Tuple\[Market, Account\]: nodes = \[ "<https://api.hive.blog>", "<https://api.hivekings.com>", "<https://anyx.io>", "<https://api.openhive.network>" \] hive<sub>instance</sub> = Hive(nodes=nodes, keys=\[active<sub>key</sub>\]) account = Account(account<sub>name</sub>, blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=hive<sub>instance</sub>) market = Market("HIVE:HBD", blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=hive<sub>instance</sub>) return market, account

def run<sub>bot</sub>(market: Market, account: Account, dryfire: bool, cycle<sub>speed</sub>: int) -\> None: print("Beginning trade cycle") print("Dryfire mode enabled." if dryfire else "LIVE TRADING MODE!!!")

try: while True: console.print("Trade cycle beginning…\[/bold cyan\]") trade<sub>cycle</sub>(market, account, dryfire) time.sleep(cycle<sub>speed</sub>) except KeyboardInterrupt: console.print("Shutting down…\[/yellow\]")

def parse<sub>args</sub>() -\> Tuple\[bool, str, Optional\[str\], Optional\[str\]\]: parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Hive Trading Bot") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–dryfire", type=str, default="true") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–live", type=str, default="false") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–account", type=str) parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–key", type=str) parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–cleanup", type=str, default="none")

args = parser.parse<sub>args</sub>() dryfire = not (args.dryfire.lower() == "false" and args.live.lower() == "true") cleanup<sub>mode</sub> = args.cleanup.lower() account<sub>name</sub> = args.account or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACCOUNT</sub>') active<sub>key</sub> = args.key or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACTIVEKEY</sub>')

return dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, active<sub>key</sub>

def main(): dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, active<sub>key</sub> = parse<sub>args</sub>()

if not account<sub>name</sub> or not active<sub>key</sub>: console.print("\[red\]Error: Hive account name and active key are required.\[/red\]") return

market, account = create<sub>bot</sub>(active<sub>key</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>) try: run<sub>bot</sub>(market, account, dryfire, CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub>) finally: cleanup(market, account, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, dryfire)

if <u><u>name</u></u> == "<u><u>main</u></u>": main()

\`\`\`

\## 0.0.3 \`\`\`python

\#!/usr/bin/env python3 import os import time import logging import argparse import json from datetime import datetime from typing import Dict, Any, Optional, List, Tuple, Callable from functools import partial, reduce from beem import Hive from beem.account import Account from beem.market import Market from rich.console import Console import pandas as pd import psycopg2 from psycopg2.extras import Json from decimal import Decimal

TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub> = {

'MIN<sub>SPREAD</sub>': 0.001, \# Minimum spread between ask and bid to consider trading 'VWAP<sub>MINUTES</sub>': 5, \# Number of minutes for VWAP calculation (minimum 5) 'MAX<sub>ASKVWAPSPREAD</sub>': 0.000, \# Maximum allowable spread between ask and VWAP 'MIN<sub>TRADEAMOUNTHBD</sub>': 0.001, \# Minimum trade amount in HBD 'SELL<sub>PRICEINCREMENT</sub>': 0.001, \# How much above buy price to place sell order

'ASK<sub>DEPTH</sub>': 5, \# How many ask orders to consider 'BID<sub>DEPTH</sub>': 5, \# How many bid orders to consider

'CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub>': 15, \# Seconds between trade cycles 'MAX<sub>ORDERS</sub>': 10 \# Maximum number of open orders allowed }

def decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(obj): if isinstance(obj, Decimal): return float(obj) elif isinstance(obj, datetime): return obj.isoformat() elif isinstance(obj, dict): return {k: decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(v) for k, v in obj.items()} elif isinstance(obj, list): return \[decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(x) for x in obj\] return obj

CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub> = TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub>'\] console = Console()

def setup<sub>logger</sub>() -\> logging.Logger: logger = logging.getLogger('bot') logger.setLevel(logging.INFO)

console<sub>handler</sub> = logging.StreamHandler() console<sub>handler</sub>.setLevel(logging.INFO) console<sub>formatter</sub> = logging.Formatter('%(message)s') console<sub>handler</sub>.setFormatter(console<sub>formatter</sub>)

file<sub>handler</sub> = logging.FileHandler('bot<sub>dev</sub>.log') file<sub>handler</sub>.setLevel(logging.DEBUG) file<sub>formatter</sub> = logging.Formatter('%(asctime)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s') file<sub>handler</sub>.setFormatter(file<sub>formatter</sub>)

logger.addHandler(console<sub>handler</sub>) logger.addHandler(file<sub>handler</sub>) return logger

def setup<sub>database</sub>() -\> psycopg2.extensions.connection: database<sub>url</sub> = os.environ.get('DATABASE<sub>URL</sub>') if not database<sub>url</sub>: raise ValueError("DATABASE<sub>URL</sub> environment variable not set") conn = psycopg2.connect(database<sub>url</sub>)

with conn.cursor() as cur:

cur.execute(""" CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS market<sub>history</sub> ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, timestamp TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT<sub>TIMESTAMP</sub>, trade<sub>data</sub> JSONB ) """) cur.execute(""" CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS orderbook<sub>history</sub> ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, timestamp TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT<sub>TIMESTAMP</sub>, orderbook<sub>data</sub> JSONB ) """) cur.execute(""" CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS account<sub>orders</sub> ( id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY, timestamp TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT<sub>TIMESTAMP</sub>, order<sub>data</sub> JSONB ) """) conn.commit() return conn

def store<sub>markethistory</sub>(conn: psycopg2.extensions.connection, trades: List\[Dict\]) -\> None: with conn.cursor() as cur: cur.execute( "INSERT INTO market<sub>history</sub> (trade<sub>data</sub>) VALUES (%s)", (Json(decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(trades)),) ) conn.commit()

def store<sub>orderbook</sub>(conn: psycopg2.extensions.connection, orderbook: Dict) -\> None: with conn.cursor() as cur: cur.execute( "INSERT INTO orderbook<sub>history</sub> (orderbook<sub>data</sub>) VALUES (%s)", (Json(decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(orderbook)),) ) conn.commit()

def store<sub>accountorder</sub>(conn: psycopg2.extensions.connection, order: Dict) -\> None: with conn.cursor() as cur: cur.execute( "INSERT INTO account<sub>orders</sub> (order<sub>data</sub>) VALUES (%s)", (Json(decimal<sub>tofloat</sub>(order)),) ) conn.commit()

def get<sub>orderbook</sub>(market: Market, logger: logging.Logger) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: try: orderbook = market.orderbook() logger.debug(f"Retrieved orderbook: {orderbook}") return orderbook except Exception as e: logger.error(f"Error retrieving orderbook: {str(e)}") return None

def analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>(orderbook: Optional\[Dict\], logger: logging.Logger) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if not orderbook or 'asks' not in orderbook or 'bids' not in orderbook: return None

lowest<sub>ask</sub> = float(orderbook\['asks'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) highest<sub>bid</sub> = float(orderbook\['bids'\]\[0\]\['price'\]) spread = lowest<sub>ask</sub> - highest<sub>bid</sub>

logger.info(f"Market Status - Ask: {lowest<sub>ask</sub>:.3f}, Bid: {highest<sub>bid</sub>:.3f}, Spread: {spread:.3f}")

return {'ask': lowest<sub>ask</sub>, 'bid': highest<sub>bid</sub>, 'spread': spread}

def get<sub>tradehistory</sub>(market: Market, logger: logging.Logger) -\> Optional\[List\]: try: trades = market.market<sub>history</sub>(1, TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['VWAP<sub>MINUTES</sub>'\] \* 60, 0) logger.debug(f"Retrieved trade history: {trades}") return trades except Exception as e: logger.error(f"Error retrieving trade history: {str(e)}") return None

def process<sub>trade</sub>(trade: Dict) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if 'hive' not in trade or 'non<sub>hive</sub>' not in trade: return None

hive<sub>data</sub> = trade\['hive'\] non<sub>hive</sub> = trade\['non<sub>hive</sub>'\]

if float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]) \<= 0: return None

return { 'price': float(non<sub>hive</sub>\['close'\]) / float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['close'\]), 'hive<sub>volume</sub>': float(hive<sub>data</sub>\['volume'\]) }

def calculate<sub>vwap</sub>(trades: Optional\[List\], logger: logging.Logger) -\> Optional\[float\]: if not trades: logger.info("No trades in the last 3 minutes") return None

processed<sub>trades</sub> = list(filter(None, map(process<sub>trade</sub>, trades))) if not processed<sub>trades</sub>: return None

df = pd.DataFrame(processed<sub>trades</sub>) vwap = (df\['price'\] \* df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\]).sum() / df\['hive<sub>volume</sub>'\].sum() logger.info(f"Calculated VWAP: {vwap:.3f}") return vwap

def get<sub>balances</sub>(account: Account, logger: logging.Logger) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: try: balances = { 'HIVE': float(account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HIVE')), 'HBD': float(account.get<sub>balance</sub>('available', 'HBD')) } logger.info(f"Account Balances - HIVE: {balances\['HIVE'\]:.3f}, HBD: {balances\['HBD'\]:.3f}") return balances except Exception as e: logger.error(f"Error retrieving balances: {str(e)}") return None

def analyze<sub>opportunity</sub>( market<sub>status</sub>: Optional\[Dict\], vwap: Optional\[float\], balances: Optional\[Dict\], logger: logging.Logger ) -\> Optional\[Dict\]: if not all(\[market<sub>status</sub>, vwap, balances\]): return None

vwap<sub>spread</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - vwap logger.info(f"VWAP Analysis - Current: {vwap:.3f}, Spread to Ask: {vwap<sub>spread</sub>:.3f}")

if not (market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] \<= vwap and vwap<sub>spread</sub> \<= TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['MAX<sub>ASKVWAPSPREAD</sub>'\]): return None

spread = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] - market<sub>status</sub>\['bid'\] if spread \<= TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['MIN<sub>SPREAD</sub>'\]: return None

buy<sub>price</sub> = market<sub>status</sub>\['ask'\] sell<sub>price</sub> = buy<sub>price</sub> + TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['SELL<sub>PRICEINCREMENT</sub>'\] min<sub>tradehive</sub> = TRADE<sub>CONFIG</sub>\['MIN<sub>TRADEAMOUNTHBD</sub>'\] / (sell<sub>price</sub> - buy<sub>price</sub>) required<sub>hbd</sub> = min<sub>tradehive</sub> \* buy<sub>price</sub>

if balances\['HIVE'\] \< min<sub>tradehive</sub> or balances\['HBD'\] \< required<sub>hbd</sub>: logger.info(f"Insufficient funds - Need {min<sub>tradehive</sub>:.3f} HIVE and {required<sub>hbd</sub>:.3f} HBD") return None

return {'buy': buy<sub>price</sub>, 'sell': sell<sub>price</sub>, 'volume': min<sub>tradehive</sub>}

def execute<sub>trade</sub>( market: Market, opportunity: Dict, account: Account, logger: logging.Logger, db<sub>conn</sub>: psycopg2.extensions.connection ) -\> None: buy<sub>order</sub> = market.buy(opportunity\['buy'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=account) sell<sub>order</sub> = market.sell(opportunity\['sell'\], opportunity\['volume'\], account=account)

logger.info(f"Orders placed - Volume: {opportunity\['volume'\]:.3f} HIVE") store<sub>accountorder</sub>(db<sub>conn</sub>, {'buy': buy<sub>order</sub>, 'sell': sell<sub>order</sub>})

def cleanup(market: Market, account: Account, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>: str, dryfire: bool, logger: logging.Logger) -\> None: try: if cleanup<sub>mode</sub> == 'all' and not dryfire: logger.info("Cancelling all open orders…") market.cancel<sub>all</sub>(account=account) logger.info("All orders cancelled") except Exception as e: logger.error(f"Error during cleanup: {str(e)}")

def trade<sub>cycle</sub>( market: Market, account: Account, dryfire: bool, logger: logging.Logger, db<sub>conn</sub>: psycopg2.extensions.connection ) -\> None: logger.info("Trade cycle beginning…")

orderbook = get<sub>orderbook</sub>(market, logger) if orderbook: store<sub>orderbook</sub>(db<sub>conn</sub>, orderbook)

market<sub>status</sub> = analyze<sub>orderbook</sub>(orderbook, logger)

trades = get<sub>tradehistory</sub>(market, logger) if trades: store<sub>markethistory</sub>(db<sub>conn</sub>, trades)

vwap = calculate<sub>vwap</sub>(trades, logger) balances = get<sub>balances</sub>(account, logger)

opportunity = analyze<sub>opportunity</sub>(market<sub>status</sub>, vwap, balances, logger)

if opportunity and not dryfire: execute<sub>trade</sub>(market, opportunity, account, logger, db<sub>conn</sub>) elif opportunity: logger.info("Dryfire mode enabled. Moving on.") else: logger.info("No trading opportunity found")

def create<sub>bot</sub>(active<sub>key</sub>: str, account<sub>name</sub>: str) -\> Tuple\[Market, Account\]: nodes = \[ "<https://api.hive.blog>", "<https://api.hivekings.com>", "<https://anyx.io>", "<https://api.openhive.network>" \] hive<sub>instance</sub> = Hive(nodes=nodes, keys=\[active<sub>key</sub>\]) account = Account(account<sub>name</sub>, blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=hive<sub>instance</sub>) market = Market("HIVE:HBD", blockchain<sub>instance</sub>=hive<sub>instance</sub>) return market, account

def run<sub>bot</sub>(market: Market, account: Account, dryfire: bool, cycle<sub>speed</sub>: int, logger: logging.Logger, db<sub>conn</sub>: psycopg2.extensions.connection) -\> None: logger.info("Beginning trade cycle") logger.info("Dryfire mode enabled." if dryfire else "LIVE TRADING MODE!!!")

try: while True: trade<sub>cycle</sub>(market, account, dryfire, logger, db<sub>conn</sub>) time.sleep(cycle<sub>speed</sub>) except KeyboardInterrupt: logger.info("Shutting down…")

def parse<sub>args</sub>() -\> Tuple\[bool, str, Optional\[str\], Optional\[str\]\]: parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Hive Trading Bot") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–dryfire", type=str, default="true") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–live", type=str, default="false") parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–account", type=str) parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–key", type=str) parser.add<sub>argument</sub>("–cleanup", type=str, default="none")

args = parser.parse<sub>args</sub>() dryfire = not (args.dryfire.lower() == "false" and args.live.lower() == "true") cleanup<sub>mode</sub> = args.cleanup.lower() account<sub>name</sub> = args.account or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACCOUNT</sub>') active<sub>key</sub> = args.key or os.environ.get('HIVE<sub>ACTIVEKEY</sub>')

return dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, active<sub>key</sub>

def main(): logger = setup<sub>logger</sub>() try: db<sub>conn</sub> = setup<sub>database</sub>() except Exception as e: logger.error(f"Failed to setup database: {str(e)}") return

dryfire, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>, active<sub>key</sub> = parse<sub>args</sub>()

if not account<sub>name</sub> or not active<sub>key</sub>: logger.error("Error: Hive account name and active key are required.") return

market, account = create<sub>bot</sub>(active<sub>key</sub>, account<sub>name</sub>) try: run<sub>bot</sub>(market, account, dryfire, CYCLE<sub>SPEED</sub>, logger, db<sub>conn</sub>) finally: cleanup(market, account, cleanup<sub>mode</sub>, dryfire, logger) db<sub>conn</sub>.close()

if <u><u>name</u></u> == "<u><u>main</u></u>": main()

\`\`\`

# HiveMeta

HiveMeta is a decentralized application on the Hive blockchain designed for community-driven curation of account metadata. Using proof-of-stake, users can delegate Hive Power (HP) to the HiveMeta Curation Bot (MCB) to propose and vote on metadata tags for Hive accounts, such as “tip bot,” “curation account,” or “vaporware.” Voting power grows progressively with the duration of HP delegation, ensuring that only sustained participants have significant influence, while undelegation triggers a gradual decay of voting rights to discourage opportunistic behavior.

Metadata changes are time-locked for review, remaining provisional for 30 days to prevent manipulation and allow for disputes. Proposal fees, paid in HBD, discourage spam and fund rewards for long-term delegators and accurate voters. All metadata, proposals, and votes are transparently stored on-chain, enabling easy integration with other Hive dApps. HiveMeta fosters trust and accountability within the Hive ecosystem, empowering the community to collaboratively enhance transparency and improve the user experience.

# HLDSHBD

HLDSHBD is a liquid token derived from pooled staked HBD. The token is managed by a smart contract that stakes all deposited HBD, earning rewards that are automatically compounded back into the pool. HLDSHBD represents a proportional claim on the pool, including both the staked principal and accrued rewards. The token can be traded or integrated into DeFi protocols, offering holders liquidity and yield simultaneously. Redemption is facilitated by sending HLDSHBD to the contract, which unstakes and returns the corresponding share of the pool’s value to the sender.H

After too many years with a [Chromebook](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/chromebook.md) and limited [Internet access](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/services/computer-web-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/internet-access.md) as my normal computer environment, I'm back on a ["real" laptop](../../../../../../../../../../content/text/documents/articles/biographies/emsenn/emsenns-omnibook.md) with a real [Internet connection](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/internet-connection.md), and that means a return to [Emacs](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/emacs.md). There's a lot of ways to do that; this is what felt like the simplest:

The first step in this process is [installing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/installing.md) the [Windows Subsystem for Linux](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/windows-subsystem-for-linux.md). To do that, I opened [Windows Powershell](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/windows-powershell.md) and input the command \`wsl –install\` which according to the output, downloaded WSL 2.5.9, installed [Ubuntu](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/ubuntu.md), and went ahead and started it up for me, prompting me to input a username and password.

From there, I was already in a command-line interface for the subsystem, so ran \`sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade\`. (To open the [CLI](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/computer-systems-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/command-line-interface.md) if it's not already, I run the "Ubuntu" app.)

This next step was habit: \`sudo apt install build-essentials curl git\`

Turns out [Git](../../../../../../../../../../../engineering-services-section/services/computer-engineering-services-section/services/computer-code-engineering-services-section/services/computer-code-development-services-section/services/version-control-services-section/services/git-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/git.md) and curl were already installled, and it's not like I plan on building Emacs from source, but now I have build-essentials installed for if/when I need it.

The next step is all that's actually necessary: \`sudo apt install emacs\`

This gave me a prompt to set up a mail system; I selected the option for local only, as (for now) that is true. (Though the prompt affirmed an urgency of sorting out my domain names and mail systems.)

There is one more thing I want to do, and that's symlink the \`~/org\` folder I'll put most of my digital content in with the matching folder in my Windows' users My Documents folder: \`ln -s /mnt/c/Users/emsenn/Documents/org ~/org\`

# Tracks

## "ADHD" {#c34e7690-4227-425f-88f8-fce0a6385cc5}

[music track](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/music-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/07/21/2025-07-21-music-track.md), [electronic](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/electronic.md), [metal](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/metal.md)

***Inclusion*** is the [condition](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/condition.md) [induced](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/inducing.md) by [including](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/including.md). It describes whether a [thing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thing.md) is held within what is; it is true when some thing is present within the [domain of relating](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/domain-of-relating.md).

## Derivation

- [deriving](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/deriving.md) [existing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/existing.md) determines that a thing:
  - Be included in what it is (inducing inclusion)
  - Be [excluded](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/excluding.md) from what it is not (inducing [exclusion](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/exclusion.md))
- The [act](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/concepts/tekodynamics-concepts-act-act.md) of including induces the condition of inclusion:
  - This condition describes the thing as a [member](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/member.md) of a set.
- [Deriving](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/deriving.md) [existing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/existing.md) requires that a [thing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thing.md):
  - Be included in what it is (inducing inclusion);
  - And be excluded from what it is not (inducing [exclusion](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/exclusion.md)).

## Derived Truths

- Every [thing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thing.md) that [exists](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/existing.md) is [conditioned](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/conditioning.md) by both [inclusion](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/concepts/inclusion.md) and [exclusion](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/exclusion.md).

***Inequivalent*** is the [condition](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/condition.md) of a [thing](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/thing.md) being [not](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/math-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/negation.md)-[equivalent](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/equivalent.md) to another thing.

# Draft 1

*Why a thing needs to relate to exist*

We usually think of things—like people, countries, ideas, or even cells—as solid, self-contained units. They just “are.” But when you look closer, that’s not really how anything works. Relational dynamics is a way of understanding how things stay coherent—not by being isolated, but by constantly relating to everything around them.

## What Is Relational Dynamics?

Relational dynamics is a formal framework that says: for something to exist in a stable way, it has to actively maintain its boundaries. That means it has to keep including what belongs to it and excluding what doesn’t. And it has to keep doing this over time—not just once.

Think of a circle drawn on paper. It’s only a circle because of the line that separates it from the rest of the page. Erase the line, and the circle disappears. That’s the basic idea: things don’t exist as “things-in-themselves”; they exist because they’re constantly performing the work of staying distinct.

## Why Is This Different from Other Theories?

Relational dynamics builds on ideas from structuralism and post-structuralism.

Structuralism (like Saussure in linguistics) says that meaning comes from difference—like how a word means something because it’s not another word. But structuralism treats systems as fixed grids. Everything has its place, and that’s that.

Post-structuralism (like Derrida or Foucault) challenges that idea. It says meaning is unstable and always shifting. Relational dynamics agrees that things aren’t fixed, but instead of just pointing out the instability, it shows how systems actively work to stay coherent—even as they change.

So relational dynamics isn’t just about what things are. It’s about what they have to do to keep being what they are.

## Reflexivity: Thinking About Your Own Boundaries

Once a system (like a person, a country, or a concept) has figured out how to stay coherent, it faces a new challenge: how to keep that process going. This is where reflexivity comes in. Reflexivity means the system starts to relate to its own way of relating.

For example:

- A legal system doesn’t just make laws—it makes rules about how laws can be made.
- A person doesn’t just have thoughts—they can think about their thoughts and change them.
- A theory doesn’t just describe things—it can describe how it describes things.

This recursive step is crucial. Without it, systems can’t adapt or evolve. Reflexivity helps them stabilize their own stabilization.

## Why It Matters

Relational dynamics isn’t just abstract philosophy. It applies to real life. Every time you:

- Change your mind about something,
- Set boundaries in a relationship,
- Organize a group or a project,
- Or even just decide what kind of person you want to be,

…you’re doing relational work. You’re including some things, excluding others, and sometimes reflecting on how you’re doing that.

Relational dynamics gives us a way to understand that process clearly. It shows that persistence—whether of a person, a community, or an idea—isn’t automatic. It’s something we have to keep doing.

## Final Thoughts

Relational dynamics teaches us that existence isn’t passive. It’s active. To be something—to stay coherent—we have to relate, reflect, and revise. There’s no final state of being, no perfect balance that lasts forever. But there is a logic to how coherence works, and relational dynamics helps us see it.

In a world where everything is changing—identities, systems, beliefs—this framework gives us a way to understand how things hold together, and what it takes to keep them from falling apart.

# Draft 2

We often think of things as ****self-contained units****: a person, a thought, a nation, a cell. We assume these things simply <u>are</u>. But a closer look reveals something more intriguing: everything that persists, everything that holds together as a recognizable "something," does so not because it is ****inherently self-sufficient****, but because it is ****maintaining coherence through active relation****.

Relational dynamics is a formal framework built around this core insight: ****existence itself is a continuous act of relating.**** Whether we’re talking about a physical object, a social system, or a conceptual category, each of these must engage in an ongoing process of \*\*including what it is and excluding what it is not\*\*—not once, but over and over, to stay coherent.

At first glance, this may seem intuitive. After all, we often describe social identities or ecosystems in terms of their ****interconnections****. A friendship network, a legal system, a biological organism—all these ****persist through interaction****. But relational dynamics goes deeper: it formalizes the ****underlying logic**** that makes such persistence possible at any level of reality. It gives us a ****grammar of coherence****, showing not just <u>that</u> things relate, but ****how relation itself is structured, maintained, and deepened over time.****

****What Does “Relational Dynamics” Actually Mean?****

At its most basic, relational dynamics starts with a simple principle: for something to exist coherently, it must be ****distinguished from what it is not.**** This distinction isn’t passive. It must be ****actively maintained.**** Think of a circle drawn on a page. What makes it a circle? The line that marks where it begins and ends—the act of enclosing some space and excluding the rest.

This seems almost trivial, but it contains the germ of a powerful idea: the circle doesn’t exist as a “thing-in-itself” apart from its ****boundary relation.**** Erase the line, and the circle ceases to exist as a distinct form.

We can scale up from this simple image. A nation exists not just because of its people or land, but because it ****continuously enacts its borders—through laws, defense, culture.**** A personal identity forms and persists by continuously integrating new experiences that “fit” while rejecting or reframing those that don’t.

In each case, we see the same pattern: existence requires ****ongoing acts of inclusion and exclusion.**** But crucially, relational dynamics shows that these acts are not merely occasional; they are ****the very fabric of persistence.****

****Moving from Static to Dynamic: Why This Isn’t Just Structuralism****

This idea might feel reminiscent of ****structuralism****, which posits that things gain meaning through their place in a larger system of differences. A word, for instance, has meaning because it’s not some other word. But traditional structuralism often portrays these systems as ****static grids****: each element in its assigned slot, held in place by difference.

Relational dynamics shifts the emphasis from ****static structure to active process.**** It’s not enough to have drawn the circle once; the circle must be ****continuously enacted.**** A cell isn’t alive because its membrane <u>once</u> formed; it’s alive because it ****actively maintains its boundary****, repairing tears, exchanging materials, keeping itself in dynamic balance with its environment.

This means that coherence is not a one-time achievement; it’s a ****recurring performance.**** And this is where relational dynamics begins to carve out its distinctive space: it is not simply about ****where things stand**** but about ****what they must continually do to stand at all.****

****Deepening the Picture: Reflexivity as the Engine of Persistence****

But there’s more. Once a relational unit—whether a cell, a nation, or a concept—has stabilized its inclusion and exclusion, it faces a new challenge: ****how to stabilize the very mechanisms of its stabilization.****

This is where ****reflexivity**** enters the scene. Reflexivity means that a system starts to ****relate to its own boundary.**** The initial act of marking “what I am” and “what I’m not” becomes ****an object of its own engagement.**** A legal system, for instance, doesn’t just create laws; it creates ****meta-laws—rules about how laws can be made and changed.**** Or think of the mind’s capacity for ****self-reflection****: we don’t merely have thoughts; we can think <u>about</u> our thoughts, adjusting, refining, or critiquing them.

In relational dynamics, reflexivity isn’t just an interesting add-on—it’s essential. Any system that aims to ****persist over time and complexity**** must develop a way to ****stabilize how it stabilizes.**** Without this recursive step, coherence eventually frays.

Here, readers familiar with ****Hegelian dialectics**** might recognize a kind of echo: a process where each stage of development encounters its own limit and must be transcended. And there’s truth in that. But relational dynamics is importantly different: where Hegel’s dialectic moves toward a ****final synthesis****, a closure in Absolute Spirit, relational dynamics insists that ****no closure is ever final.**** Every time coherence is achieved—whether at the level of simple relation or meta-reflexive structure—it exposes ****new dynamics**** that must be formalized in turn.

****Minimalism with Precision: The Art of Just Enough****

Another standout feature of relational dynamics is its ****minimalist rigor.**** Each new dynamic—be it reflexivity, composition, or network-building—is introduced only when logically necessary, and always with ****the simplest structure that suffices**** to stabilize coherence at that stage.

This may remind you of \*\*Occam’s razor\*\*—the idea that the simplest solution is usually best. But here it’s applied ****not just to ideas but to the formal architecture of existence itself.**** It’s a philosophy of ****structural economy****: nothing extra, nothing ornamental, just enough to hold coherence in place.

This approach contrasts with traditions like ****mystical holism**** or even ****postmodern deconstruction****, which can either dissolve distinctions into oneness or revel in endless fragmentation. Relational dynamics avoids both extremes. It recognizes that coherence is ****fragile and finite****, requiring continuous care, but it also insists that each stage of coherence must be \*\*formally secured\*\*—no vague gestures, no unstructured flows.

****Where Familiar Ideas Overlap—and Where They Don’t****

In science, you might find echoes of this in ****complexity theory****, where systems self-organize and maintain coherence through feedback loops. Or in ****information theory****, where messages are defined by the exclusion of other possibilities (a bit being 0 rather than 1). But these fields generally assume the \*\*system itself is already given\*\*—a network with nodes and edges, a communication channel ready to transmit.

Relational dynamics steps back a level: it asks ****what it takes to constitute the very coherence of a system in the first place.**** It formalizes not just <u>how systems behave</u> but <u>how systems come to be, maintain themselves, and deepen through recursion.</u>

Similarly, there’s a kinship with ****Buddhist emptiness****, which teaches that things have no independent existence but arise through interdependent co-arising. Yet relational dynamics doesn’t collapse into formlessness. Every destabilization (a moment of "emptiness") is followed by a ****new minimal formalization,**** ensuring that coherence is rebuilt, not just dissolved.

****A Practical Reflection: This Is About Us, Too****

It’s easy to imagine this as abstract philosophy, but its implications are tangible. Every time you ****navigate a personal change, hold together a team, rethink a belief, or manage a boundary in a relationship,**** you are enacting relational dynamics. You are performing the work of ****coherence: including, excluding, reflecting, revising.****

This framework offers a lens to see that process ****clearly and formally.**** It shows that persistence—whether of ideas, communities, or selves—is not a static state but an ongoing labor of ****relation and reflexivity.****

****In Closing: The Ongoing Work of Coherence****

Relational dynamics gives us more than a theory; it gives us a ****blueprint for understanding persistence itself.**** It reveals that to exist is to engage in ****relational work,**** to stabilize what we are while staying open to what we must become. Every boundary we draw—whether around a thought, a system, or a self—is not a terminus but a ****starting point for deeper relation.****

In a world of shifting identities, evolving systems, and recursive challenges, this framework offers both ****clarity and humility:**** clarity about what it takes to maintain coherence, and humility in recognizing that the work is ****never finally done.****

## "White Van" {#dcfeada1-a82d-47cc-ab42-1dbf33a513bc}

[music track](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/music-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/07/21/2025-07-21-music-track.md), [punk](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/punk.md)

This [letter to the Web](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/services/agency-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/undated/letter-to-the-web.md) is for folk who, so far, have experienced most of the year through [headline](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/headline.md) and [social media discourse](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/social-media-discourse.md).

Things are worse than it’s easy to believe. [Unemployment is worse](https://www.lisep.org/). [Death tolls are higher](https://www.cdc.gov/mmwr/volumes/69/wr/mm6942e2.htm). Anecdotally, there are four new [homeless camps](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/homeless-camp.md) within a half mile of me, all bigger than the one that existed before [COVID](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/covid-19-syndemic.md), and more than a dozen people have [dying](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/services/relationality-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/dying.md) in these camps. (Reminder, I live in a very prosperous [liberal university town](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-and-economic-geography-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/liberal-university-town.md).) I [distribute](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/distributing.md) now a couple hundred pounds of [food](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/food.md) a day, and it still isn’t enough to make a meaningful dent.

If you don’t feel, acutely, the rapid slide into [poverty](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/poverty.md) that has occurred for most of our nation: congratulations, you have more time to prepare. Please, I’m begging you: take advantage of it to by listening to your impoverished [neighbors](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/neighbor.md) and [community](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/community.md), and taking steps to work with them directly. \*\*Regardless\*\* of who wins the election, there are many million new Americans existing in a state of subjugation. If you aren’t one of them, you’re the subjugator, and I implore you to do everything you can to change that relationship.

You all have a week to figure out how you’re going to react to the various possible outcomes of the U.S. elections. You’re probably waking up to news about a new Supreme Court Justice, and that the Senate went on recess before passing additional relief. Social media discussion about how this threatens reproductive rights, and puts people on the precipice of eviction.

Please try and understand: most of my friends weren’t able to access reproducive healthcare, before COVID. Even before Trump. It’s never been available for them. (I am the healthcare available to many of my peers.) Most of my friends can’t qualify for leases because they’ve been evicted too often. \*I\* don’t have healthcare, or the means to qualify for a lease, and haven’t for \*almost a decade\*. (It’s only in the past five years that I’ve had any sort of stable housing, and at the moment it isn’t stable at all; my landlord/boss is actively trying to convince the sheriff to ignore the eviction moritorium because they dislike that I distribute free food.)

The things you’re scared of, the risks you’re facing, have been here for years for some of us: and not distant abstract “other Americans,” but like, me and my friends. People you know. People whose efforts you benefit from: not just in that we keep providing the services that maintain your society, but we keep providing words like this, that help alleviate the ignorances y’all hold around your privilege.

These consequences you’re waiting on, to motivate you to action, have already come for the majority of your fellow citizens, and it is your continued support of our oppression that inhibits our ability to organize.

I’m not going to spend much time guilting you for relaxing into the stability that Collaboration provided so far. There’s enough of that in my other writings, and from other people. What I’m going to challenge you to do is make clear plans about how you’re going to make this world better, regardless of how the election goes. Come Wednesday morning, are you going to fight your HOA to allow gardens to be set up? Are you going to go talk to a restaurant about donating waste food? Reach out to local mutualists and ask if they need help?

Do real things that directly challenge the many mechanisms of oppression which you’re currently cooperating with. “Releasing an open source blog engine” or “boosting some non-white social media posts” shouldn’t count. This is real shit, and it requires real action.

So yesterday’s newsletter about [planning for the future](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/political-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/social-sciences/governing/content/politics/archive/letter-to-the-web-20201027-yallve-a-week.org.md) upset a sizeable portion of my small readership, enough that I got multiple complaints. I was told that it, and my newsletter so far, have been “unnecessarily antagonistic” by multiple people.

To everyone who felt the same flurry of emotions but didn’t encourage me to talk about something else: thank you. But the response makes me think, maybe I should explain what this newsletter is. After all, some of you got subscribed automatically from Patreon, and others might not have read the \[about page\](../../../../research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/applied-sciences/technology/archive/library/org-mode/references/repositories/emsenn-org-mode/<https:/emsenn.substack.com/about>).

\[

\![\](../../../../research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/applied-sciences/technology/archive/library/org-mode/references/repositories/emsenn-org-mode/<https:/substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!sZ65>!,w<sub>1456</sub>,c<sub>limit</sub>,f<sub>auto</sub>,q<sub>auto</sub>:good,fl<sub>progressive</sub>:steep/https:/bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/2c7a0fbb-a6a4-4f62-8cbc-ff6ef33d25f6<sub>792x538</sub>.jpeg)

\](../../../../research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/applied-sciences/technology/archive/library/org-mode/references/repositories/emsenn-org-mode/<https:/substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!sZ65>!,f<sub>auto</sub>,q<sub>auto</sub>:good,fl<sub>progressive</sub>:steep/https:/bucketeer-e05bbc84-baa3-437e-9518-adb32be77984.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/2c7a0fbb-a6a4-4f62-8cbc-ff6ef33d25f6<sub>792x538</sub>.jpeg)

That page encourages a development of knowledge about certain topics, among them <sub>decolonization</sub>\_, both through this newsletter, conversations we have, and external resources. To help y’all with that, I’m going to share some items tagged <sub>\*</sub>\*decolonization\*\*\_ from my reading list. Some of these will be academic, some casual; some very broad and some specific.

Here's your reading list\](../../../../research/2026/07/31/lenticule-import/library/applied-sciences/technology/archive/library/org-mode/references/repositories/emsenn-org-mode/<https:/theconversation.com/amp/want-to-understand-the-decolonisation-debate-heres-your-reading-list-51279>)
