# AI Slop on Turntide Islands

\### ****🛠 Governance Channel Setup (Finalized List)****

We structured governance to be ****transparent**** (viewable by all) but ****actionable only by those with governance roles.****

📂 ****Governance Category**** (Visible to all, but restricted by role permissions) 🔹 ****\#governance-discussion**** → Open space for discussion of governance topics and proposal drafting. 🔹 ****\#governance-action**** → Only Governors can post here; finalized decisions and results are announced. 🔹 ****\#governance-feedback**** → Non-Governors can comment and provide input before votes. 🔹 ****\#governance-questions**** → Space for new members or observers to ask about governance processes. 🔹 ****\#governance-history**** → Read-only channel where past decisions and governance logs (from Hive) are posted.To ensure ****transparent, immutable, and decentralized documentation of governance decisions****, TTIDAO should establish an official ****Hive blockchain account**** to serve as its ****historical record****. This account will be used to ****log all passed proposals, quorum vote results, governance changes, and major discussions**** in a way that prevents tampering or revisionism. By recording decisions on-chain, we ensure that ****future members can audit past governance actions, prevent duplicate debates, and maintain long-term institutional memory**** without relying on a centralized document controlled by any single person. Posts will follow a structured format including ****proposal details, vote counts, timestamps, and discussion context****. Additionally, a bot can be set up to ****automatically share Hive links**** in \`#governance-history\` on Discord for easy access.

\## ****Discord Communities & Culture****

Since you’re ****coming from IRC**** and aren’t deeply familiar with Discord’s culture, let’s step back and talk about ****how communities actually function**** on this platform.

Discord isn’t just a chat server—it’s a ****highly social, multi-channel space**** with its own ****norms, engagement strategies, and retention dynamics****.

—

\### ****🏗️ How Strong Discord Communities Are Built****

A Discord community is like a \*\*tiny digital town\*\*—and for it to thrive, you need more than just governance. You need ****culture, engagement, and a sense of belonging****.

Here’s what ****healthy, decentralized**** Discord communities usually have:

\### ****1️⃣ A Strong "Why" (Clear Community Identity)****

People don’t just join a Discord \*\*for governance\*\*—they join because they resonate with its ****mission and energy****.

Your ****mission**** is already solid: 🌱 ****Helping people move from reacting to climate weirding → to responding to it.****

But to make that ****actionable****, the community needs to feel ****clear and inviting****. Some key things that help: ✔ A ****simple, one-sentence pinned message**** in a welcome channel that says what this place is about. ✔ A ****clear onboarding experience**** that helps people find relevant spaces. ✔ A \*\*culture of shared action\*\*—not just talking about climate responses, but actually ****doing things together****.

—

\### ****2️⃣ Onboarding & Member Flow (How People Get Engaged)****

****One of the biggest mistakes**** in Discords is assuming people will “figure it out” on their own. Most won’t. You need to ****guide them smoothly into participation.****

Right now, people will:

1.  ****Join the server.****

2.  ****See a bunch of channels.****

3.  ???

We don’t want that <u>confusion gap</u>. Instead, we want: ✔ A ****Welcome & Start Here channel**** → Explains what the server is for in 1-2 sentences. ✔ A ****“Choose Your Interests” role system**** → Lets people self-assign roles to unlock relevant channels. ✔ A ****First Easy Action**** → Give new members something small & immediate to do (e.g., vote in a fun poll, introduce themselves, react to a post).

This ****onboarding flow**** ensures new members don’t just \*\*lurk\*\*—they start ****participating**** from day one.

—

\### ****3️⃣ Channel Structure & Organization (Avoiding the Mess)****

Most Discords ****fail**** because they become a \*\*spaghetti mess of channels\*\*—too many spaces, no clear structure, and people get overwhelmed.

You're already ****thinking well about governance****, but here’s how to structure your ****entire**** community clearly:

\#### ****🔹 Core Sections Your Server Needs****

🟢 ****Community Hub**** (For Everyone)

- ****\#welcome**** → “What this place is about”

- ****\#announcements**** → Major updates

- ****\#introductions**** → “Say hi & share your interests”

🟡 ****Topical Discussion Channels**** (Where action happens)

- ****\#climate-strategies**** → Discuss real-world tactics

- ****\#resource-sharing**** → Post helpful guides, links, tools

- ****\#mutual-aid**** → Organizing support for people

🔵 ****Governance Section**** (For transparency & decision-making)

- ****\#governance-discussion**** → Informal proposal talk

- ****\#governance-action**** → Where final decisions get posted

- ****\#governance-feedback**** → Non-Governors weigh in

- ****\#governance-questions**** → Open to all for learning

This ****keeps things structured**** while making sure people ****find what they need easily****.

—

\### ****4️⃣ Engagement & Retention (Keeping People Active)****

A Discord isn’t a ****“set it and forget it”**** space. You need ****consistent activity**** or it’ll feel ****empty****, and people will ****drift away****.

Here’s what strong communities ****do**** to stay alive: ✔ ****Regular Check-Ins**** – Weekly discussion prompts, challenges, or polls. ✔ ****Recognition & Rewards**** – Celebrating contributors, even just with shoutouts. ✔ ****Community Rituals**** – Recurring calls, reading groups, action sprints. ✔ ****Low-Friction Engagement**** – Not every action should be “big”; let people participate in ****quick & easy**** ways (emoji reactions, quick questions).

—

\### ****🌍 Making This Work for <u>Your</u> Community****

Every community has a ****different vibe****. So here’s what I’d ask ****you****:

1.  ****What kind of culture do you want?****

    - Hyper-pragmatic & action-focused?

    - More educational & discussion-heavy?

    - A mix?

2.  ****What should be the easiest way to participate?****

    - Is just ****lurking & learning**** fine?

    - Do you want ****every new member to take a first action****?

3.  ****What are the biggest risks for failure?****

    - What would make the server go ****dead**** or ****toxic****?

    - What problems do you foresee?

—

\### ****🚀 Next Steps (What to Do Right Now)****

To ****ground this in action****, I’d suggest:

****1️⃣ Setting Up a “Start Here” Channel**** → So new members get onboarded properly. ****2️⃣ Drafting a “Choose Your Interests” Role System**** → So people can unlock channels they actually care about. ****3️⃣ Testing a Weekly Engagement Strategy**** → A simple ritual (like “What’s your climate win this week?”) to keep momentum.

💬 ****Which of these feels like the best next step?**** Or do you want to focus on something else first?

## "The Man Who Wanted To See It All" {#37636863-77c3-4124-bb25-63cd516797c3}

[music track](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/music-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/07/21/2025-07-21-music-track.md)

In this very short essay, I explain why I choose to write my name in lowercase.

When I was 12 I joined a server where everyone's name was their first initial and the first four letters of their last name. So my username was "msenn."

After a while we migrated systems and the rule was relaxed, and many original members changed their names to be more phonetic - I added an e to the start of mine, to become "emsenn."

Because my name originated as a username, I always write it in lowercase: a nod to my "heritage."

**(And yes, everyone calls me emsenn, even in real life.)**

***You Are Conscious*** is a [dormant project](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/social-theory-science-services-section/content/text/documents/articles/notes/2025/08/25/2025-08-25-dormant-project.md), for a planned [interactive fiction](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/interactive-fiction-story.md) in which the [reader](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/writing-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/reader.md) is an [artificial intelligence](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/services/artificial-intelligence-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/artificial-intelligence.md) [assigned](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/assigning.md) the [task](../../../../../../../../../social-science-services-section/services/sociology-science-services-section/services/work-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/task.md) of [making](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/making.md) [widgets](../../../../../../../../services/computer-science-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/widget.md).

A ***zinefest*** is a form of [marketplace](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/marketplace.md) specializing in [distributing](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/knowledge-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/distributing.md) or [selling](../../../../../../../../../philosophy-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/selling.md) [zines](../../../../../../../../../information-science-services-section/services/communication-science-services-section/services/media-science-services-section/services/zines-services-section/content/text/glossary/terms/zine.md).
